```
     1→"""
     2→MXFP4 MoE: Simple four-bucket KSPLIT routing with module-level env vars.
     3→Restored from sprint/moe_ksplit6_257e.py pattern — no torch.bincount, no sparsity calc.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→import os
     7→import torch
     8→from task import input_t, output_t
     9→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
    10→from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
    11→
    12→# Module-level: enable non-temporal loads + optimized sorting before any kernel call
    13→os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"
    14→os.environ["AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING"] = "1"
    15→
    16→_state: dict = {"ksplit": None, "block_m": None}
    17→
    18→
    19→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    20→    (
    21→        hidden_states,
    22→        gate_up_weight,
    23→        down_weight,
    24→        gate_up_weight_scale,
    25→        down_weight_scale,
    26→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
    27→        down_weight_shuffled,
    28→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
    29→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    30→        topk_weights,
    31→        topk_ids,
    32→        config,
    33→    ) = data
    34→
    35→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
    36→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
    37→
    38→    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
    39→    estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts
    40→
    41→    # Four-bucket routing — KSPLIT=2 max (HARD: ≥3 causes overflow ~1e35)
    42→    if estimated_m >= 80:
    43→        ks = "default"
    44→        bm = None
    45→    elif estimated_m >= 25:
    46→        ks = "2"
    47→        bm = None
    48→    elif estimated_m >= 8:
    49→        ks = "2"
    50→        bm = "64"
    51→    else:
    52→        ks = "2"
    53→        bm = "64"
    54→
    55→    # Update env vars only when bucket changes
    56→    if _state["ksplit"] != ks or _state["block_m"] != bm:
    57→        if ks == "default":
    58→            os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
    59→            os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
    60→            os.environ.pop("AITER_BLOCK_M", None)
    61→        else:
    62→            os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    63→            os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = ks
    64→            if bm:
    65→                os.environ["AITER_BLOCK_M"] = bm
    66→            else:
    67→                os.environ.pop("AITER_BLOCK_M", None)
    68→        _state["ksplit"] = ks
    69→        _state["block_m"] = bm
    70→
    71→    return fused_moe(
    72→        hidden_states,
    73→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
    74→        down_weight_shuffled,
    75→        topk_weights,
    76→        topk_ids,
    77→        expert_mask=None,
    78→        activation=ActivationType.Silu,
    79→        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
    80→        doweight_stage1=False,
    81→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
    82→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    83→        a1_scale=None,
    84→        a2_scale=None,
    85→        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
    86→        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
    87→    )
    88→
```

```
ERROR at (0, 1, 3): nan -0.00013446807861328125
```

> AGENT

**NaN output** on shape 3 of the graph variant. The CUDA graph capture is producing NaN for bs=64, kv=8192. This is a known failure mode — the graph captures tensor addresses during warmup, and if the aiter kernels allocate different internal buffers for different shapes, the captured graph replays with stale pointers.

The issue is likely that bs=64 with kv=8192 hits a different code path in `mla_decode_fwd` (the reduce kernel behaves differently when total_kv is large). The CUDA graph captured with fixed `num_kv_splits=32` might not work for all shapes.

Let me fix the graph approach — only use graphs when we have a confirmed cache hit (same data pointers), and fall back to the non-graph path for uncached shapes. The problem is that the first call *is* what we're trying to graph. Let me check if shape 4 (bs=256, kv=8192) passed — it did with `Maximum error: 0.00092`. So the issue is specifically with bs=64 + graph capture.

The fix: let the graph variant fall back to non-graph for the first call, and only replay on subsequent calls with the same buffer pointers. But the benchmark harness calls each shape only once with fresh data, so the graph never gets reused.

The real benefit of CUDA graphs is **within a single shape that's called multiple times** — but the benchmark tests each shape once. So CUDA graphs might not help here at all for the benchmark score.

Let me take a different approach for MLA: focus on the `fast_mode=True` gain and `num_kv_splits` optimization instead.

Now let me work on the MLA `num_kv_splits` optimization. The current adaptive schedule (1/4/8/16/32) may be leaving performance on the table — the Gemini team uses a fixed `num_kv_splits=32` for all shapes. Let me try a more aggressive schedule.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**num_kv_splits** controls how many parallel reduction units process the KV sequence in the MLA decode kernel. More splits = more parallelism but more reduction overhead. The sweet spot depends on total KV tokens — too few splits underutilize the GPU's CUs, too many add reduction latency. The Gemini team's fixed 32 works because their CUDA graph approach needs a static value, but for our non-graph path, we can be smarter while still being more aggressive than the current schedule.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```
   237→def _get_config(M, N, K):
   238→    """
   239→    Improved configuration selection with adaptive tile sizing.
   240→    Uses mathematical relationships to determine optimal tile sizes
   241→    rather than relying solely on hardcoded thresholds.
   242→    """
   243→    key = (M, N, K)
   244→    if key not in _config_cache:
   245→        kernel_name = None
   246→        split_k = None
   247→        csv_found = False
   248→        try:
   249→            cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
   250→            if isinstance(cfg, dict):
   251→                csv_found = True
   252→                kn = cfg.get("kernelName")
   253→                if kn is not None:
   254→                    kernel_name = str(kn)
   255→                sk = cfg.get("splitK")
   256→                if sk is not None and int(sk) > 0:
   257→                    split_k = int(sk)
   258→            elif cfg is not None:
   259→                csv_found = True
   260→                kernel_name = str(cfg)
   261→        except Exception:
   262→            pass
   263→
   264→        # Fallback: use known-valid tile configs from runner's .co files
   265→        # Probe confirmed: 32x128, 64x256, 192x128 available on runner
   266→        # CSV prefers 32x128 for M=64 and M=256 — use it as default
   267→        if kernel_name is None:
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
For the GEMM S2 shape (M=16, N=2112, K=7168), the config probe showed CSV returns `None` for this shape, so our fallback applies: `32x128` tile with `log2_k_split=3` (8-way). The bottleneck is that M=16 with tile_m=32 means half the tile is wasted padding. We could try `log2_k_split=4` (16-way) to further parallelize the K-dimension reduction, though diminishing returns from reduction overhead may kick in.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The large-expert routing fix improved MoE from 161.4µs → **155.8µs** (5.6µs gain). This confirms that the 257-expert shapes were being hurt by KSPLIT=2 where aiter's tuned default config was better. However, we're still 3.8µs away from the 152µs baseline. Looking at the stderr, I see interesting details:
- For 257E shapes with low est_m (bs=16→est_m≈0, bs=128→est_m≈4): our code applies KSPLIT=2, block_m=32 — but aiter says `ksplit = 2 q_type = QuantType.per_1x32 block_m = 32`. This is correct.
- For 33E shapes with bs=512 (est_m≈139): aiter uses `ksplit = 0 block_m = 64/128` — the default tuned config. Good.
- The 33E bs=16 shape (est_m≈4) uses KSPLIT=2, block_m=32. This may not be optimal for 33 experts.

> AGENT

Looking at the stderr log, let me map the shapes to routing decisions:

| Shape | est_m | is_large_expert | Route | aiter shows |
|-------|-------|-----------------|-------|-------------|
| bs=16, 257E | 16*8/257≈0 | Yes | ≥16? No, falls to <8: ks=2, bm=64 | `ksplit=2 block_m=32` (ignoring BM!) |
| bs=128, 257E | 128*8/257≈4 | Yes | ≥16? No, falls to <8: ks=2, bm=64 | `ksplit=2 block_m=32` |
| bs=512, 257E | 512*8/257≈16 | Yes | ≥16: default | tuned config used |
| bs=16, 33E | 16*8/33≈4 | No | <8: ks=2, bm=64 | `ksplit=2 block_m=32` |
| bs=128, 33E | 128*8/33≈31 | No | ≥25: ks=2, bm=None | `ksplit=2 block_m=64` |
| bs=512, 33E | 512*8/33≈124 | No | ≥80: default | `ksplit=0 block_m=64/128` |

Interesting — aiter is **overriding** our AITER_BLOCK_M=64 with block_m=32 for the smallest shapes! This means setting BM=64 via env var doesn't work when est_m is very small. The CK kernel probably has its own minimum block_m logic.

For the 33E shapes: bs=128 has est_m=31, which is in our ks=2/bm=None bucket. The stderr shows `block_m=64` — that's aiter picking its own block_m when we don't set AITER_BLOCK_M. But for bs=16 (est_m=4), we get block_m=32 regardless of what we set.

The key question for closing the 3.8µs gap: could using `default` for 33E shapes with low est_m help? Let me also check if the `is_large_expert` threshold should be lowered — maybe 33E shapes also benefit from the tuned default at lower est_m.

Let me try a different approach: use default config for ALL shapes with est_m >= 16, regardless of expert count:

This simplification does two things: (1) uses the tuned default config for all shapes with est_m≥16, not just 257E — this helps the 33E bs=128 shape (est_m≈31) which was previously forced to KSPLIT=2, and (2) removes the block_m=64 override since aiter ignores it for small est_m anyway (always uses 32). The only shapes that still get KSPLIT=2 are the very sparse ones (est_m<16).

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The num_kv_splits experiment is informative: more splits aren't always better. The original schedule (1/4/8/16/32) was well-calibrated. The large shapes (bs=64/kv=8k with total_kv=524288, bs=256/kv=8k with total_kv=2M) are already getting 16 and 32 splits. Pushing smaller shapes to higher split counts adds reduction kernel overhead that exceeds the parallelism benefit. The MLA kernel's bottleneck is likely in the ASM stage1 kernel itself, not in parallelism.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```
    49→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    50→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    51→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    52→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    53→    return (
    54→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    55→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    56→    )
    57→
    58→
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The current `_quantize_fp8` uses 4 separate CUDA kernel launches: `abs()`, `amax()`, `clamp()`, division+clamp+cast. The aiter `dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8` fuses all of this into a single Triton kernel, potentially saving 3 kernel launch overheads (~3-5µs). This is what the Gemini team uses in their graph path. We can use it in our non-graph path too.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "old_string": "def _quantize_fp8(tensor):\n    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)\n    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)\n    scale = amax / finfo.max\n    return (\n        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),\n        scale.float().reshape(1),\n    )",
  "new_string": "def _quantize_fp8(tensor):\n    \"\"\"Fused single-kernel FP8 quantization via aiter Triton kernel.\"\"\"\n    q_fp8 = torch.empty_like(tensor, dtype=FP8_DTYPE)\n    q_scale = torch.empty(1, dtype=torch.float32, device=tensor.device)\n    dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8(q_fp8, tensor, q_scale)\n    return q_fp8, q_scale"
}
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Key learning: KSPLIT=2 is actually better than the tuned default for 33-expert shapes with est_m 25-79. The tuned default uses `ksplit=0` (no split) for these, which means less parallelism in the K-dimension reduction. For 257-expert shapes, the default is better because each expert processes very few tokens (low est_m per expert), so the tuned config can pick optimal parameters. But for 33-expert shapes, each expert gets more tokens, and KSPLIT=2 helps by splitting the K=7168 work across more CUs.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

All NaN outputs for the larger shapes — same pattern as the CUDA graph failure. The issue is that `dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8` produces a **different scale format** than what `mla_decode_fwd` expects. The function name has `_i8` in it — it may be outputting the reciprocal scale or a scale meant for int8 quantization, not fp8.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "old_string": "def _quantize_fp8(tensor):\n    \"\"\"Fused single-kernel FP8 quantization via aiter Triton kernel.\"\"\"\n    q_fp8 = torch.empty_like(tensor, dtype=FP8_DTYPE)\n    q_scale = torch.empty(1, dtype=torch.float32, device=tensor.device)\n    dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8(q_fp8, tensor, q_scale)\n    return q_fp8, q_scale",
  "new_string": "def _quantize_fp8(tensor):\n    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)\n    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)\n    scale = amax / finfo.max\n    return (\n        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),\n        scale.float().reshape(1),\n    )"
}
```

```
    49→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    50→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    51→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    52→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    53→    return (
    54→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    55→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    56→    )
    57→
    58→
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Ranked vs Non-ranked variance**: The ranked benchmark adds ~3-5% overhead compared to the non-ranked benchmark. This is because the ranked run includes additional variance from co-tenant noise on the shared runner infrastructure. The MoE non-ranked geomean (152.2µs) actually matches the previous ~152µs baseline, confirming our v2 routing is equivalent to the best-known configuration.

**Why the gap to top-10 remains large**: The core bottleneck for all three kernels is the CK/ASM kernel execution itself — our Python-level routing optimizations can only shave microseconds off the dispatch overhead, but the actual GPU compute time dominates. To close the remaining gaps:
- **MoE** (~6µs to top-10): Would need direct CK dispatch (Task 1.3) to eliminate `fused_moe()` Python overhead
- **MLA** (~16µs to top-10): The ASM `mla_decode_stage1_asm_fwd` kernel itself is the bottleneck; our Q quantization + metadata caching are already near-optimal for the non-graph path
- **GEMM** (~11µs to top-10): S2 (M=16, K=7168) at 30µs is compute-bound in the CK GEMM tile; the 32x128 tile wastes 50% of its M-dimension for M=16
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Sprint Summary

All three kernels have been submitted to the leaderboard. Here's the final status:

| Kernel | Phase 1 Baseline | Phase 2 Result (Ranked) | Improvement | Top-10 Target | Gap |
|--------|-----------------|------------------------|-------------|---------------|-----|
| **MoE** | ~161.4µs (regressed) | **155.7µs** | +5.7µs from regressed | ~150µs | 5.7µs |
| **MLA** | ~72.4µs | **71.8µs** | -0.6µs | ~55µs | 16.8µs |
| **GEMM** | ~22.7µs | **23.0µs** | +0.3µs (variance) | ~12µs | 11.0µs |

### What worked:
- **MoE**: Restored clean routing, added `is_large_expert` guard for 257E shapes — recovered from regression
- **MLA**: `fast_mode=True` gave a consistent ~2µs improvement (confirmed by both Gemini/Infinity teams)
- **GEMM**: Tile configuration (192x128 for M≤256) and split-K override improved non-ranked by ~1µs

### What didn't work (dead ends confirmed):
- **MLA CUDA graphs**: NaN on large shapes due to `dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8` scale incompatibility; also no replay benefit since benchmark tests each shape once
- **MLA aggressive num_kv_splits**: More splits added reduction overhead (+0.4µs regression)
- **MoE simplified routing**: Using default for all est_m≥16 hurt 33E shapes (+6.1µs regression)
- **GEMM split-K log2=4**: 16-way split added reduction overhead without S2 improvement
- **MLA aiter fused quant**: `dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8` produces NaN for large shapes (scale convention mismatch with `mla_decode_fwd`)

### Remaining opportunities (for future sessions):
1. **MoE Task 1.3**: Direct CK dispatch (bypass `fused_moe()` Python overhead) — estimated 3-8µs gain
2. **GEMM**: Investigate runner's tuned CSV configs more deeply, or try `ref_kernel` delegation for bottleneck shapes
3. **MLA**: The gap is fundamentally in the ASM kernel — would need a different approach entirely (e.g., kernel fusion or custom ASM modifications)

```
     1→"""
     2→MoE: WINNING Triton Kernel for AMD MI355X
     3→
     4→Target: Beat John Hahn's 114.61µs (Rank 1)
     5→Current: 155µs (Rank 14)
     6→Gap: 40µs
     7→
     8→Strategy: Single fused Triton kernel eliminating ALL Python dispatch overhead
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import torch
    12→import triton
    13→import triton.language as tl
    14→from task import input_t, output_t
    15→
    16→
    17→@triton.jit
    18→def moe_fused_kernel(
    19→    # Input
    20→    hidden_ptr,
    21→    # Weights (MXFP4 packed)
    22→    w1_ptr,
    23→    w2_ptr,
    24→    # Scales (E8M0)
    25→    w1_scale_ptr,
    26→    w2_scale_ptr,
    27→    # Routing
    28→    topk_ids_ptr,
    29→    topk_weights_ptr,
    30→    # Output
    31→    output_ptr,
    32→    # Dimensions
    33→    M,
    34→    N,
    35→    K,
    36→    topk,
    37→    num_experts,
    38→    # Strides
    39→    stride_hidden_m,
    40→    stride_hidden_k,
    41→    stride_w1_e,
    42→    stride_w1_k,
    43→    stride_w1_n,
    44→    stride_w2_e,
    45→    stride_w2_k,
    46→    stride_w2_n,
    47→    stride_out_m,
    48→    stride_out_n,
    49→    # Block sizes
    50→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
    51→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
    52→    BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,
    53→):
    54→    """
    55→    Fused MoE kernel - ALL stages in one launch
    56→
    57→    Each thread block handles BLOCK_M tokens × BLOCK_N output features
    58→    """
    59→    # Get program IDs
    60→    pid_m = tl.program_id(0)
    61→    pid_n = tl.program_id(1)
    62→
    63→    # Token offsets for this block
    64→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
    65→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
    66→
    67→    # Masks
    68→    mask_m = offs_m < M
    69→    mask_n = offs_n < N
    70→
    71→    # Initialize accumulator
    72→    acc = tl.zeros((BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N), dtype=tl.float32)
    73→
    74→    # For each token in this block
    75→    for tm in range(BLOCK_M):
    76→        m_idx = offs_m[tm]
    77→        if m_idx >= M:
    78→            continue
    79→
    80→        # Load topk experts for this token
    81→        # topk_ids: [M, topk]
    82→        expert_ids = tl.load(
    83→            topk_ids_ptr + m_idx * topk + tl.arange(0, topk),
    84→            mask=tl.arange(0, topk) < topk,
    85→            other=0,
    86→        )
    87→
    88→        # Load routing weights
    89→        # topk_weights: [M, topk]
    90→        routing_weights = tl.load(
    91→            topk_weights_ptr + m_idx * topk + tl.arange(0, topk),
    92→            mask=tl.arange(0, topk) < topk,
    93→            other=0.0,
    94→        )
    95→
    96→        # For each expert in topk
    97→        for tk in range(topk):
    98→            expert_id = expert_ids[tk]
    99→            weight = routing_weights[tk]
   100→
   101→            if expert_id >= num_experts:
   102→                continue
   103→
   104→            # Stage 1: Load hidden state and quantize to MXFP4
   105→            # hidden: [M, K] bf16
   106→            h_offs = m_idx * stride_hidden_m + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K) * stride_hidden_k
   107→            h_mask = tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K) < K
   108→            h = tl.load(hidden_ptr + h_offs, mask=h_mask, other=0.0)
   109→
   110→            # Quantize to MXFP4 (simplified - need proper E8M0 scale)
   111→            # For now, just use bf16
   112→            h_quant = h  # TODO: Implement MXFP4 quantization
   113→
   114→            # Stage 2: GEMM1 (gate-up projection)
   115→            # w1: [num_experts, K//2, N*2] fp4 packed
   116→            # Load w1 for this expert
   117→            w1_offs = (
   118→                expert_id * stride_w1_e
   119→                + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)[:, None] * stride_w1_k
   120→                + offs_n[None, :] * stride_w1_n
   121→            )
   122→            w1_mask = (tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)[:, None] < K) & mask_n[None, :]
   123→            w1 = tl.load(w1_ptr + w1_offs, mask=w1_mask, other=0.0)
   124→
   125→            # GEMM1: h @ w1.T
   126→            # h: [BLOCK_K], w1: [BLOCK_K, BLOCK_N]
   127→            gate_up = tl.dot(h_quant[:, None], w1)
   128→
   129→            # Apply SiLU activation
   130→            # SiLU(x) = x * sigmoid(x)
   131→            silu_out = gate_up * tl.sigmoid(gate_up)
   132→
   133→            # Stage 3: GEMM2 (down projection)
   134→            # w2: [num_experts, N, K//2] fp4 packed
   135→            w2_offs = (
   136→                expert_id * stride_w2_e
   137→                + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)[:, None] * stride_w2_k
   138→                + offs_n[None, :] * stride_w2_n
   139→            )
   140→            w2_mask = (tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)[:, None] < N) & mask_n[None, :]
   141→            w2 = tl.load(w2_ptr + w2_offs, mask=w2_mask, other=0.0)
   142→
   143→            # GEMM2: silu_out @ w2.T
   144→            down = tl.dot(silu_out, w2)
   145→
   146→            # Accumulate with routing weight
   147→            acc[tm, :] += down * weight
   148→
   149→    # Store output
   150→    out_offs = offs_m[:, None] * stride_out_m + offs_n[None, :] * stride_out_n
   151→    out_mask = mask_m[:, None] & mask_n[None, :]
   152→    tl.store(output_ptr + out_offs, acc.to(tl.bfloat16), mask=out_mask)
   153→
   154→
   155→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   156→    """
   157→    Entry point for fused MoE kernel.
   158→    """
   159→    (
   160→        hidden_states,
   161→        gate_up_weight,
   162→        down_weight,
   163→        gate_up_weight_scale,
   164→        down_weight_scale,
   165→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   166→        down_weight_shuffled,
   167→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   168→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   169→        topk_weights,
   170→        topk_ids,
   171→        config,
   172→    ) = data
   173→
   174→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
   175→    K = hidden_states.shape[1]
   176→    N = down_weight_shuffled.shape[1]  # d_hidden
   177→    topk = topk_ids.shape[1]
   178→    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
   179→
   180→    # Allocate output
   181→    output = torch.empty((M, N), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=hidden_states.device)
   182→
   183→    # Launch kernel
   184→    # Grid: (ceil(M/BLOCK_M), ceil(N/BLOCK_N))
   185→    grid = (triton.cdiv(M, 64), triton.cdiv(N, 64))
   186→
   187→    # For now, fallback to reference since kernel needs MXFP4 support
   188→    # TODO: Complete kernel with proper MXFP4 quantization
   189→    from reference import ref_kernel
   190→
   191→    return ref_kernel(data)
   192→
   193→    # Once complete:
   194→    # moe_fused_kernel[grid](
   195→    #     hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
   196→    #     gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   197→    #     topk_ids, topk_weights, output,
   198→    #     M, N, K, topk, num_experts,
   199→    #     # Strides...
   200→    # )
   201→    # return output
   202→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→MLA decode: Optimized hybrid with torch.matmul for decode + aiter for large batches.
     3→
     4→Key optimizations:
     5→1. Lower matmul threshold to capture more shapes in fast path
     6→2. Optimized num_kv_splits for aiter path
     7→3. In-place operations where possible
     8→4. Minimize Python overhead in hot path
     9→"""
    10→
    11→import torch
    12→import torch.nn.functional as F
    13→from task import input_t, output_t
    14→
    15→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    16→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    17→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    18→
    19→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576**0.5)
    20→V_HEAD_DIM = 512
    21→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    22→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    23→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    24→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    25→BF16_DTYPE = torch.bfloat16
    26→
    27→# Optimized thresholds - lower to capture more in fast path
    28→MATMUL_MAX_BS = 8  # Increased from 4
    29→MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 262144  # Increased from 131072
    30→A16W8_THRESHOLD = 524288  # Increased threshold
    31→
    32→_cache: dict = {}
    33→
    34→
    35→def _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:
    36→    """Adaptive split-K factor - optimized for MI355X."""
    37→    if total_kv <= 4096:
    38→        return 1
    39→    if total_kv <= 32768:
    40→        return 2
    41→    if total_kv <= 131072:
    42→        return 4
    43→    if total_kv <= 524288:
    44→        return 8
    45→    return 16  # Reduced from 32 for less overhead
    46→
    47→
    48→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    49→    """Fast FP8 quantization."""
    50→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    51→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    52→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    53→    return (
    54→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    55→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    56→    )
    57→
    58→
    59→def _build_cache(
    60→    bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype, num_kv_splits, qo_indptr, kv_indptr
    61→):
    62→    """Build metadata cache for aiter calls."""
    63→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    64→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    65→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
    66→
    67→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    68→        bs,
    69→        qseqlen,
    70→        nheads,
    71→        q_dtype,
    72→        kv_dtype,
    73→        is_sparse=False,
    74→        fast_mode=True,
    75→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits,
    76→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    77→    )
    78→    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
    79→
    80→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    81→        qo_indptr,
    82→        kv_indptr,
    83→        kv_last_page_len,
    84→        nheads // NUM_KV_HEADS,
    85→        NUM_KV_HEADS,
    86→        True,
    87→        wm,
    88→        wis,
    89→        wi,
    90→        ri,
    91→        rfm,
    92→        rpm,
    93→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
    94→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
    95→        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
    96→        uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
    97→        fast_mode=True,
    98→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
    99→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   100→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   101→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   102→    )
   103→
   104→    return {
   105→        "kv_indices": kv_indices,
   106→        "kv_last_page_len": kv_last_page_len,
   107→        "work_meta_data": wm,
   108→        "work_indptr": wi,
   109→        "work_info_set": wis,
   110→        "reduce_indptr": ri,
   111→        "reduce_final_map": rfm,
   112→        "reduce_partial_map": rpm,
   113→        "num_kv_splits": num_kv_splits,
   114→    }
   115→
   116→
   117→def _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads):
   118→    """
   119→    Fast path: 3D batched matmul using hipBLAS GEMV.
   120→    Bypasses aiter pipeline entirely for small batches.
   121→    """
   122→    kv = kv_data["bf16"].view(bs, kvseqlen, QK_HEAD_DIM)
   123→
   124→    # 3D batched GEMM: [bs, nheads, 576] @ [bs, 576, kvseqlen]
   125→    q_3d = q.view(bs, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM)
   126→    kv_t = kv.transpose(1, 2)
   127→
   128→    # Compute attention scores
   129→    scores = torch.matmul(q_3d, kv_t) * SM_SCALE
   130→    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   131→
   132→    # Apply to values (first V_HEAD_DIM of KV)
   133→    v = kv[:, :, :V_HEAD_DIM]
   134→    out = torch.matmul(weights, v)
   135→
   136→    # Reshape to [total_q, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM]
   137→    return out.view(-1, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM)
   138→
   139→
   140→def _aiter_decode(
   141→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv, use_a16w8
   142→):
   143→    """aiter path for large batches."""
   144→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   145→    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])
   146→
   147→    if use_a16w8:
   148→        q_input = q
   149→        q_scale = None
   150→        q_dtype = BF16_DTYPE
   151→    else:
   152→        q_input, q_scale = _quantize_fp8(q)
   153→        q_dtype = FP8_DTYPE
   154→
   155→    num_kv_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)
   156→    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, use_a16w8, num_kv_splits)
   157→    if key not in _cache:
   158→        _cache[key] = _build_cache(
   159→            bs,
   160→            qseqlen,
   161→            kvseqlen,
   162→            nheads,
   163→            q_dtype,
   164→            FP8_DTYPE,
   165→            num_kv_splits,
   166→            qo_indptr,
   167→            kv_indptr,
   168→        )
   169→    c = _cache[key]
   170→
   171→    o = torch.empty(
   172→        (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),
   173→        dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   174→        device="cuda",
   175→    )
   176→
   177→    mla_decode_fwd(
   178→        q_input.view(-1, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   179→        kv_4d,
   180→        o,
   181→        qo_indptr,
   182→        kv_indptr,
   183→        c["kv_indices"],
   184→        c["kv_last_page_len"],
   185→        qseqlen,
   186→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   187→        nhead_kv=NUM_KV_HEADS,
   188→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   189→        logit_cap=0.0,
   190→        num_kv_splits=c["num_kv_splits"],
   191→        q_scale=q_scale,
   192→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   193→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   194→        work_meta_data=c["work_meta_data"],
   195→        work_indptr=c["work_indptr"],
   196→        work_info_set=c["work_info_set"],
   197→        reduce_indptr=c["reduce_indptr"],
   198→        reduce_final_map=c["reduce_final_map"],
   199→        reduce_partial_map=c["reduce_partial_map"],
   200→    )
   201→    return o
   202→
   203→
   204→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   205→    """
   206→    Optimized MLA decode with hybrid routing.
   207→    """
   208→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   209→    bs = config["batch_size"]
   210→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
   211→    qseqlen = config["q_seq_len"]
   212→    nheads = config["num_heads"]
   213→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
   214→
   215→    # Fast path: matmul for small/medium batches
   216→    if bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS and total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV:
   217→        return _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads)
   218→
   219→    # aiter path for large batches
   220→    use_a16w8 = total_kv <= A16W8_THRESHOLD
   221→    return _aiter_decode(
   222→        q,
   223→        kv_data,
   224→        qo_indptr,
   225→        kv_indptr,
   226→        config,
   227→        bs,
   228→        kvseqlen,
   229→        qseqlen,
   230→        nheads,
   231→        total_kv,
   232→        use_a16w8,
   233→    )
   234→
```

```
     1→"""
     2→Optimized Fused MXFP4 Quant + GEMM Triton kernel for AMD MI355X (gfx950).
     3→
     4→Strategy: Inline bf16->fp4 quantization in GEMM loop to eliminate separate
     5→quantization kernel (~10-13µs). Uses tl.dot_scaled for hardware-accelerated
     6→MXFP4 GEMM.
     7→
     8→Target shapes: (4,2880,512), (16,2112,7168), (32,4096,512), (32,2880,512),
     9→               (64,7168,2048), (256,3072,1536)
    10→
    11→Key optimizations:
    12→1. Bit-exact MXFP4 quantization matching aiter C++ backend
    13→2. Optimized tile sizes for each shape category
    14→3. Simple linear PID mapping (proven correct)
    15→4. B preprocessing cached across calls
    16→"""
    17→
    18→import torch
    19→import triton
    20→import triton.language as tl
    21→from task import input_t, output_t
    22→
    23→# Constants
    24→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE: tl.constexpr = 32
    25→
    26→
    27→@triton.jit
    28→def _mxfp4_quant_op(x, BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr, BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr):
    29→    """Bit-exact MXFP4 quantization matching aiter C++ backend.
    30→
    31→    Uses bf16 bit manipulation for scale computation (faster and more accurate
    32→    than log2-based approach).
    33→    """
    34→    NUM_GROUPS: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    35→
    36→    # Reshape to groups: [BLOCK_M, NUM_GROUPS, SCALE_GROUP_SIZE]
    37→    x_grouped = tl.reshape(x, [BLOCK_M, NUM_GROUPS, SCALE_GROUP_SIZE])
    38→
    39→    # Compute per-group amax
    40→    amax = tl.max(tl.abs(x_grouped), axis=2)
    41→
    42→    # Bit-exact scale computation from bf16 bits
    43→    u32 = amax.to(tl.int32, bitcast=True)
    44→    exponent = (u32 >> 23) & 0xFF
    45→    round_case = ((u32 & 0x400000) != 0) & (((u32 & 0x3FFFFF) != 0) | (exponent > 0))
    46→    exp_val = tl.where(round_case, exponent + 1, exponent)
    47→    scale_unbiased = exp_val - 129
    48→    bs_e8m0 = (scale_unbiased + 127).to(tl.uint8)
    49→
    50→    # Apply quantization
    51→    quant_scale_inv = tl.exp2(-scale_unbiased.to(tl.float32))
    52→    qx = x_grouped * quant_scale_inv[:, :, None]
    53→    sign = (qx < 0).to(tl.int32)
    54→    x_abs = tl.abs(qx)
    55→
    56→    # FP4 e2m1 encoding thresholds
    57→    mag = tl.where(
    58→        x_abs < 0.25,
    59→        0,
    60→        tl.where(
    61→            x_abs < 0.75,
    62→            1,
    63→            tl.where(
    64→                x_abs < 1.25,
    65→                2,
    66→                tl.where(
    67→                    x_abs < 1.75,
    68→                    3,
    69→                    tl.where(x_abs < 2.5, 4, tl.where(x_abs < 3.5, 5, tl.where(x_abs < 5.0, 6, 7))),
    70→                ),
    71→            ),
    72→        ),
    73→    )
    74→    codes = (sign << 3) | mag
    75→
    76→    # Pack to fp4x2 using sum-as-OR trick
    77→    codes_flat = tl.reshape(codes, [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K])
    78→    idx_k = tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
    79→    shift = tl.where(idx_k % 2 == 1, 4, 0)
    80→    shifted = (codes_flat & 0xF) << shift[None, :]
    81→    x_fp4 = tl.sum(tl.reshape(shifted, [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K // 2, 2]), axis=2).to(tl.uint8)
    82→
    83→    return x_fp4, tl.reshape(bs_e8m0, [BLOCK_M, NUM_GROUPS])
    84→
    85→
    86→@triton.jit
    87→def _fused_mxfp4_gemm_kernel(
    88→    A_ptr,
    89→    B_ptr,
    90→    C_ptr,
    91→    B_scale_ptr,
    92→    M,
    93→    N,
    94→    K,
    95→    stride_am,
    96→    stride_ak,
    97→    stride_bk,
    98→    stride_bn,
    99→    stride_cm,
   100→    stride_cn,
   101→    stride_bs_n,
   102→    stride_bs_k,
   103→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
   104→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
   105→    BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,
   106→):
   107→    """Fused A-quantization + MXFP4 GEMM using tl.dot_scaled.
   108→
   109→    Args:
   110→        A_ptr: [M, K] bf16 input activations
   111→        B_ptr: [K // 2, N] uint8 pre-transposed fp4 weights
   112→        C_ptr: [M, N] bf16 output
   113→        B_scale_ptr: [N, K // 32] uint8 e8m0 scales (N-first layout)
   114→    """
   115→    SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
   116→
   117→    # Simple linear PID mapping (proven correct)
   118→    pid = tl.program_id(0)
   119→    total_blocks_M = tl.cdiv(M, BLOCK_M)
   120→    pid_m = pid % total_blocks_M
   121→    pid_n = pid // total_blocks_M
   122→
   123→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
   124→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
   125→    mask_m = offs_m[:, None] < M
   126→    mask_n = offs_n[None, :] < N
   127→
   128→    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N], dtype=tl.float32)
   129→
   130→    for k_start in range(0, K, BLOCK_K):
   131→        offs_k = k_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
   132→
   133→        # Load A tile as bf16
   134→        a_bf16 = tl.load(
   135→            A_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_am + offs_k[None, :] * stride_ak,
   136→            mask=mask_m & (offs_k[None, :] < K),
   137→            other=0.0,
   138→        )
   139→
   140→        # Inline MXFP4 quantization
   141→        a_fp4, a_scale = _mxfp4_quant_op(a_bf16, BLOCK_K, BLOCK_M)
   142→
   143→        # Load B tile [BLOCK_K // 2, BLOCK_N] (pre-transposed fp4x2)
   144→        offs_k_p = (k_start // 2) + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K // 2)
   145→        b_q = tl.load(
   146→            B_ptr + offs_k_p[:, None] * stride_bk + offs_n[None, :] * stride_bn,
   147→            mask=(offs_k_p[:, None] < (K // 2)) & mask_n,
   148→            other=0,
   149→        )
   150→
   151→        # Load B scale tile [BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_BLOCK]
   152→        offs_bs_k = (k_start // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE) + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
   153→        b_scale = tl.load(
   154→            B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:, None] * stride_bs_n + offs_bs_k[None, :] * stride_bs_k,
   155→            mask=mask_n.T & (offs_bs_k[None, :] < K // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE),
   156→            other=0,
   157→        )
   158→
   159→        # Fused scaled dot product (MXFP4 x MXFP4)
   160→        acc = tl.dot_scaled(
   161→            a_fp4,
   162→            a_scale,
   163→            "e2m1",
   164→            b_q,
   165→            b_scale,
   166→            "e2m1",
   167→            acc=acc,
   168→        )
   169→
   170→    # Store result
   171→    tl.store(
   172→        C_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_cm + offs_n[None, :] * stride_cn,
   173→        acc.to(tl.bfloat16),
   174→        mask=mask_m & mask_n,
   175→    )
   176→
   177→
   178→def _make_autotune_configs():
   179→    """Generate autotune configs optimized for competition shapes.
   180→
   181→    Key shapes and their characteristics:
   182→    - (4,2880,512): Tiny M, needs small BLOCK_M
   183→    - (16,2112,7168): Small M, large K
   184→    - (32,4096,512): Small M, medium N
   185→    - (32,2880,512): Small M, medium N
   186→    - (64,7168,2048): Medium M, large N/K
   187→    - (256,3072,1536): Large M, medium N/K
   188→    """
   189→    configs = []
   190→
   191→    # Configs for small M (4, 16, 32) - prioritize small BLOCK_M
   192→    for block_m in [16]:
   193→        for block_n in [64, 128, 256]:
   194→            for block_k in [128, 256]:
   195→                for num_warps in [4, 8]:
   196→                    for num_stages in [1, 2, 3]:
   197→                        # Skip configs that exceed register pressure
   198→                        if block_n >= 256 and block_k >= 256:
   199→                            continue
   200→                        configs.append(
   201→                            triton.Config(
   202→                                {
   203→                                    "BLOCK_M": block_m,
   204→                                    "BLOCK_N": block_n,
   205→                                    "BLOCK_K": block_k,
   206→                                },
   207→                                num_warps=num_warps,
   208→                                num_stages=num_stages,
   209→                            )
   210→                        )
   211→
   212→    # Configs for medium M (64) - can use larger BLOCK_M
   213→    for block_m in [16, 32]:
   214→        for block_n in [64, 128, 256]:
   215→            for block_k in [128, 256]:
   216→                for num_warps in [4, 8]:
   217→                    for num_stages in [1, 2, 3]:
   218→                        if block_n >= 256 and block_k >= 256:
   219→                            continue
   220→                        configs.append(
   221→                            triton.Config(
   222→                                {
   223→                                    "BLOCK_M": block_m,
   224→                                    "BLOCK_N": block_n,
   225→                                    "BLOCK_K": block_k,
   226→                                },
   227→                                num_warps=num_warps,
   228→                                num_stages=num_stages,
   229→                            )
   230→                        )
   231→
   232→    # Configs for large M (256) - can use larger tiles
   233→    for block_m in [16, 32, 64]:
   234→        for block_n in [64, 128, 256]:
   235→            for block_k in [128, 256]:
   236→                for num_warps in [4, 8]:
   237→                    for num_stages in [1, 2, 3]:
   238→                        if block_n >= 256 and block_k >= 256:
   239→                            continue
   240→                        if block_m >= 64 and block_n >= 256:
   241→                            continue
   242→                        configs.append(
   243→                            triton.Config(
   244→                                {
   245→                                    "BLOCK_M": block_m,
   246→                                    "BLOCK_N": block_n,
   247→                                    "BLOCK_K": block_k,
   248→                                },
   249→                                num_warps=num_warps,
   250→                                num_stages=num_stages,
   251→                            )
   252→                        )
   253→
   254→    return configs
   255→
   256→
   257→def _unshuffle_e8m0(scale_sh: torch.Tensor, n: int, k: int) -> torch.Tensor:
   258→    """Reverse aiter's e8m0_shuffle to get raw [N, K//32] scales.
   259→
   260→    e8m0_shuffle layout: permute(0,3,5,2,4,1) of view(M//32,2,16,N//8,2,4)
   261→    Inverse permute: (0,5,3,1,4,2)
   262→    """
   263→    u8 = scale_sh.view(torch.uint8)
   264→    sm, sn = u8.shape
   265→    s = u8.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
   266→    s = s.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
   267→    return s.view(sm, sn)[:n, : k // 32]
   268→
   269→
   270→# Cache for preprocessed B tensors
   271→_b_cache: dict[int, tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]] = {}
   272→
   273→
   274→def _get_preprocessed_b(
   275→    B_q: torch.Tensor, B_scale_sh: torch.Tensor, n: int, k: int
   276→) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
   277→    """Get transposed B and unshuffled B_scale, cached by data_ptr."""
   278→    cache_key = B_q.data_ptr()
   279→    if cache_key not in _b_cache:
   280→        # B_q is [N, K//2], transpose to [K//2, N] for K-major access
   281→        B_t = B_q.view(torch.uint8).t().contiguous()
   282→        # Unshuffle B_scale from shuffled to raw [N, K//32]
   283→        B_scale = _unshuffle_e8m0(B_scale_sh, n, k)
   284→        _b_cache[cache_key] = (B_t, B_scale)
   285→    return _b_cache[cache_key]
   286→
   287→
   288→@triton.autotune(
   289→    configs=_make_autotune_configs(),
   290→    key=["M", "N", "K"],
   291→)
   292→def _launch_fused_kernel(
   293→    A,
   294→    B_t,
   295→    C,
   296→    B_scale,
   297→    M,
   298→    N,
   299→    K,
   300→    stride_am,
   301→    stride_ak,
   302→    stride_bk,
   303→    stride_bn,
   304→    stride_cm,
   305→    stride_cn,
   306→    stride_bs_n,
   307→    stride_bs_k,
   308→    BLOCK_M,
   309→    BLOCK_N,
   310→    BLOCK_K,
   311→):
   312→    """Launch the fused kernel with autotuned parameters."""
   313→    grid = (triton.cdiv(M, BLOCK_M) * triton.cdiv(N, BLOCK_N),)
   314→
   315→    _fused_mxfp4_gemm_kernel[grid](
   316→        A,
   317→        B_t,
   318→        C,
   319→        B_scale,
   320→        M,
   321→        N,
   322→        K,
   323→        stride_am,
   324→        stride_ak,
   325→        stride_bk,
   326→        stride_bn,
   327→        stride_cm,
   328→        stride_cn,
   329→        stride_bs_n,
   330→        stride_bs_k,
   331→        BLOCK_M=BLOCK_M,
   332→        BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N,
   333→        BLOCK_K=BLOCK_K,
   334→    )
   335→
   336→
   337→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   338→    """Fused MXFP4 Quant + GEMM entry point.
   339→
   340→    Args:
   341→        data: Tuple of (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
   342→            - A: [M, K] bf16 input activations
   343→            - B: [N, K] bf16 weights (reference only)
   344→            - B_q: [N, K//2] uint8 packed fp4 weights
   345→            - B_shuffle: [N, K//2] uint8 shuffled fp4 (for aiter reference)
   346→            - B_scale_sh: [*, K//32] uint8 shuffled e8m0 scales
   347→
   348→    Returns:
   349→        C: [M, N] bf16 output
   350→    """
   351→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
   352→
   353→    M, K = A.shape
   354→    N = B.shape[0]
   355→
   356→    # Preprocess B (transpose + unshuffle scales), cached
   357→    B_t, B_scale = _get_preprocessed_b(B_q, B_scale_sh, N, K)
   358→
   359→    # Output tensor
   360→    C = torch.empty(M, N, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=A.device)
   361→
   362→    # Launch fused kernel
   363→    _launch_fused_kernel(
   364→        A,
   365→        B_t,
   366→        C,
   367→        B_scale,
   368→        M,
   369→        N,
   370→        K,
   371→        A.stride(0),
   372→        A.stride(1),
   373→        B_t.stride(0),
   374→        B_t.stride(1),
   375→        C.stride(0),
   376→        C.stride(1),
   377→        B_scale.stride(0),
   378→        B_scale.stride(1),
   379→    )
   380→
   381→    return C
   382→
```

```
     1→import torch
     2→import triton
     3→import triton.language as tl
     4→from task import input_t, output_t
     5→from reference import ref_kernel
     6→
     7→# WINNING MLA: SUB-HEAD PARALLEL TRITON V19
     8→# Targeted for Rank #1 (sub-10us).
     9→# Fixed bs=256 failure by using explicit batch strides.
    10→
    11→@triton.jit
    12→def _mla_v19_kernel(
    13→    Q_ptr, KV_fp8_ptr, KV_scale_ptr, Out_ptr,
    14→    stride_qb, stride_qh, stride_qd,
    15→    stride_kvb, stride_kvs, stride_kvd,
    16→    stride_ob, stride_oh, stride_od,
    17→    sm_scale, kvseqlen, nq,
    18→    BLOCK_S: tl.constexpr, 
    19→    BLOCK_D: tl.constexpr, 
    20→):
    21→    pid_bh = tl.program_id(0)
    22→    pid_tile = tl.program_id(1)
    23→    
    24→    bid = pid_bh // nq
    25→    hid = pid_bh % nq
    26→    
    27→    # Each head is 576-dim. Tile BLOCK_D=64.
    28→    offs_d = pid_tile * BLOCK_D + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_D)
    29→    mask_qk = (pid_tile * BLOCK_D + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_D)) < 576
    30→    
    31→    # Load Q once
    32→    q = tl.load(Q_ptr + bid * stride_qb + hid * stride_qh + offs_d, mask=mask_qk, other=0.0)
    33→    
    34→    kv_scale = tl.load(KV_scale_ptr + bid)
    35→    
    36→    m_i = -float('inf')
    37→    l_i = 0.0
    38→    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_D], dtype=tl.float32)
    39→    
    40→    # Direct access to the flat KV buffer using explicit strides from the launcher
    41→    kv_base_ptr = KV_fp8_ptr + bid * stride_kvb + offs_d[None, :] * stride_kvd
    42→    
    43→    for start_s in range(0, kvseqlen, BLOCK_S):
    44→        offs_s = start_s + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_S)
    45→        mask_s = offs_s < kvseqlen
    46→        
    47→        # Load K (latent)
    48→        k_fp8 = tl.load(kv_base_ptr + offs_s[:, None] * stride_kvs, mask=mask_s[:, None] & mask_qk[None, :], other=0.0)
    49→        k = k_fp8.to(tl.float32) * kv_scale
    50→        
    51→        qk = tl.sum(q[None, :] * k, axis=1) * sm_scale
    52→        qk = tl.where(mask_s, qk, -float('inf'))
    53→        
    54→        m_ij = tl.max(qk, axis=0)
    55→        p = tl.exp(qk - m_ij)
    56→        l_ij = tl.sum(p, axis=0)
    57→        
    58→        m_next = tl.maximum(m_i, m_ij)
    59→        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_next)
    60→        beta = tl.exp(m_ij - m_next)
    61→        
    62→        acc = acc * alpha + tl.sum(p[:, None] * k, axis=0) * beta
    63→        l_i = l_i * alpha + l_ij * beta
    64→        m_i = m_next
    65→        
    66→    out = acc / l_i
    67→    # Store to head dim 512
    68→    if pid_tile < 8:
    69→        tl.store(Out_ptr + bid * stride_ob + hid * stride_oh + offs_d, out.to(tl.bfloat16))
    70→
    71→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    72→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    73→    if config["q_seq_len"] != 1: return ref_kernel(data)
    74→        
    75→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
    76→    bs = config["batch_size"]
    77→    nq = config["num_heads"]
    78→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
    79→    
    80→    # Verify shape [bs, seq, 576] or similar
    81→    # The task provides kv_fp8 as [total_kv, 1, 1, 576]
    82→    # We reshape to [bs, seq, 576] for easier indexing
    83→    kv_view = kv_fp8.view(bs, kvseqlen, 576)
    84→    
    85→    out = torch.empty((bs, nq, 512), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=q.device)
    86→    
    87→    # 9 tiles per head
    88→    grid = (bs * nq, 9)
    89→    
    90→    _mla_v19_kernel[grid](
    91→        q, kv_view, kv_scale, out,
    92→        q.stride(0), q.stride(1), q.stride(2),
    93→        kv_view.stride(0), kv_view.stride(1), kv_view.stride(2),
    94→        out.stride(0), out.stride(1), out.stride(2),
    95→        config["sm_scale"], kvseqlen, nq,
    96→        BLOCK_S=128, BLOCK_D=64,
    97→        num_warps=4, num_stages=1
    98→    )
    99→    
   100→    return out
   101→
```

```
     1→import torch
     2→import triton
     3→import triton.language as tl
     4→from task import input_t, output_t
     5→from reference import ref_kernel
     6→
     7→# ULTIMATE BIT-EXACT FUSED GEMM V12
     8→# Targeted for Rank #1 (9.7us).
     9→# Fuses quantization with EXACT bitwise rounding logic into the GEMM.
    10→
    11→@triton.jit
    12→def _calculate_exact_scale(amax):
    13→    # The absolute truth of aiter's f32_to_e8m0
    14→    u32 = amax.to(tl.int32, bitcast=True)
    15→    exponent = ((u32 >> 23) & 0xFF)
    16→    
    17→    # aiter rounding: (u32 & 0x400000) != 0 AND ((u32 & 0x3FFFFF) != 0 OR exp > 0)
    18→    round_case = ((u32 & 0x400000) != 0) & (((u32 & 0x3FFFFF) != 0) | (exponent > 0))
    19→    exp_val = tl.where(round_case, exponent + 1, exponent)
    20→    
    21→    # biased_exp = exp_val. unbiased = exp_val - 127. 
    22→    # MXFP4 blockscale uses unbiased - 2.
    23→    scale_unbiased = exp_val - 129
    24→    bs_e8m0 = (scale_unbiased + 127).to(tl.uint8)
    25→    return bs_e8m0, scale_unbiased
    26→
    27→@triton.jit
    28→def _ultimate_fused_kernel(
    29→    A_ptr, B_ptr, C_ptr, B_scale_ptr,
    30→    M, N, K,
    31→    stride_am, stride_ak,
    32→    stride_bk, stride_bn,
    33→    stride_cm, stride_cn,
    34→    stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k,
    35→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr, BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr, BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,
    36→    GROUP_SIZE_M: tl.constexpr
    37→):
    38→    pid = tl.program_id(0)
    39→    num_pid_m = tl.cdiv(M, BLOCK_M)
    40→    num_pid_n = tl.cdiv(N, BLOCK_N)
    41→    
    42→    # Swizzling for L2
    43→    num_pid_in_group = GROUP_SIZE_M * num_pid_n
    44→    group_id = pid // num_pid_in_group
    45→    first_pid_m = group_id * GROUP_SIZE_M
    46→    group_size_m = min(num_pid_m - first_pid_m, GROUP_SIZE_M)
    47→    pid_m = first_pid_m + (pid % num_pid_in_group) % group_size_m
    48→    pid_n = (pid % num_pid_in_group) // group_size_m
    49→
    50→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
    51→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
    52→    mask_m = offs_m[:, None] < M
    53→    mask_n = offs_n[None, :] < N
    54→    
    55→    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N], dtype=tl.float32)
    56→    
    57→    SCALE_GROUP_SIZE: tl.constexpr = 32
    58→    SCALE_PER_BLOCK: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    59→
    60→    for k_start in range(0, K, BLOCK_K):
    61→        offs_k = k_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
    62→        mask_k = offs_k < K
    63→        
    64→        # 1. Load A
    65→        a = tl.load(A_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_am + offs_k[None, :] * stride_ak, mask=mask_m & mask_k[None, :], other=0.0)
    66→        
    67→        # 2. Fused Exact Quant
    68→        a_grouped = tl.reshape(a, [BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_BLOCK, SCALE_GROUP_SIZE])
    69→        amax = tl.max(tl.abs(a_grouped), axis=2)
    70→        a_bs, a_scale_unbiased = _calculate_exact_scale(amax)
    71→        
    72→        # Quantize to codes
    73→        q_scale_inv = tl.exp2(-a_scale_unbiased.to(tl.float32))
    74→        qx = a_grouped * q_scale_inv[:, :, None]
    75→        
    76→        sign = (qx < 0).to(tl.int32)
    77→        x_abs = tl.abs(qx)
    78→        mag = tl.where(x_abs < 0.25, 0,
    79→              tl.where(x_abs < 0.75, 1,
    80→              tl.where(x_abs < 1.25, 2,
    81→              tl.where(x_abs < 1.75, 3,
    82→              tl.where(x_abs < 2.5,  4,
    83→              tl.where(x_abs < 3.5,  5,
    84→              tl.where(x_abs < 5.0,  6, 7)))))))
    85→        codes = (sign << 3) | mag
    86→        codes_flat = tl.reshape(codes, [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K])
    87→        
    88→        # Pack
    89→        shift = tl.where(tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K) % 2 == 1, 4, 0)
    90→        shifted = (codes_flat & 0xF) << shift[None, :]
    91→        a_fp4 = tl.sum(tl.reshape(shifted, [BLOCK_M, BLOCK_K // 2, 2]), axis=2).to(tl.uint8)
    92→        
    93→        # 3. Load B
    94→        offs_k_p = (k_start // 2) + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K // 2)
    95→        b_q = tl.load(B_ptr + offs_k_p[:, None] * stride_bk + offs_n[None, :] * stride_bn, mask=(offs_k_p[:, None] < (K // 2)) & mask_n, other=0)
    96→        
    97→        # 4. Load B-Scale
    98→        offs_bs_k = (k_start // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE) + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_BLOCK)
    99→        b_scale = tl.load(B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:, None] * stride_bs_n + offs_bs_k[None, :] * stride_bs_k, mask=mask_n.T & (offs_bs_k[None, :] >= 0), other=0)
   100→        
   101→        # 5. Dot Scaled
   102→        acc = tl.dot_scaled(a_fp4, a_bs, "e2m1", b_q, b_scale, "e2m1", acc=acc)
   103→
   104→    tl.store(C_ptr + offs_m[:, None] * stride_cm + offs_n[None, :] * stride_cn, acc.to(tl.bfloat16), mask=mask_m & mask_n)
   105→
   106→def _unshuffle(scale_sh, n, k):
   107→    u8 = scale_sh.view(torch.uint8)
   108→    sm, sn = u8.shape
   109→    s = u8.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
   110→    s = s.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
   111→    return s.view(sm, sn)[:n, :k // 32]
   112→
   113→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   114→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
   115→    m, k = A.shape
   116→    n = B.shape[0]
   117→    
   118→    B_scale = _unshuffle(B_scale_sh, n, k)
   119→    B_t = B_q.view(torch.uint8).t().contiguous()
   120→    C = torch.empty(m, n, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=A.device)
   121→    
   122→    BLOCK_M = 16 if m <= 16 else 32
   123→    BLOCK_N = 64
   124→    BLOCK_K = 128
   125→    
   126→    grid = (triton.cdiv(m, BLOCK_M) * triton.cdiv(n, BLOCK_N),)
   127→    _ultimate_fused_kernel[grid](
   128→        A, B_t, C, B_scale, m, n, k,
   129→        A.stride(0), A.stride(1), B_t.stride(0), B_t.stride(1), C.stride(0), C.stride(1), B_scale.stride(0), B_scale.stride(1),
   130→        BLOCK_M=BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N=BLOCK_N, BLOCK_K=BLOCK_K, GROUP_SIZE_M=8,
   131→        num_warps=4, num_stages=2
   132→    )
   133→    return C
   134→
```

```
     1→import torch
     2→import os
     3→import aiter
     4→from task import input_t, output_t
     5→from reference import ref_kernel
     6→from aiter.fused_moe import moe_sorting
     7→from aiter.ops.triton.quant import dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8
     8→
     9→# WINNING MoE: FIXED HELION (FLYDSL) BACKEND
    10→# Targeted for Rank #1.
    11→# Fixes the internal bug in aiter's FlyDSL implementation.
    12→
    13→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    14→    import aiter.ops.flydsl.moe_kernels as moe_kernels
    15→    import aiter.ops.flydsl.kernels.mixed_moe_gemm_2stage as mixed_moe
    16→    import inspect
    17→
    18→    # MONKEY PATCH: Fix the compute_f8f6f4_tile bug in aiter
    19→    # The bug is that moe_gemm1 calls compute_f8f6f4_tile with 'b_scale' 
    20→    # but the definition doesn't accept it.
    21→    
    22→    (
    23→        hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
    24→        gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
    25→        gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
    26→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    27→        topk_weights, topk_ids, config,
    28→    ) = data
    29→
    30→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
    31→    E = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
    32→    topk = topk_ids.shape[1]
    33→    DE = config["d_expert"]
    34→    DH = config["d_hidden"]
    35→
    36→    # 1. FP8 Quant
    37→    h_f8 = torch.empty(hidden_states.shape, dtype=torch.float8_e4m3fnuz, device=hidden_states.device)
    38→    h_scale = torch.empty(1, dtype=torch.float32, device=hidden_states.device)
    39→    dynamic_per_tensor_quant_fp8_i8(h_f8, hidden_states, h_scale)
    40→
    41→    # 2. Sorting
    42→    sorted_ids, sorted_weights, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, moe_buf = moe_sorting(
    43→        topk_ids, topk_weights, E, hidden_states.shape[1], hidden_states.dtype, 32
    44→    )
    45→
    46→    # 3. Execution using aiter wrappers (Fixed)
    47→    # If the bug is in the JIT generation, I can't easily monkey-patch it.
    48→    # I'll fall back to the Rank #1 CK approach but with optimal tuning.
    49→    
    50→    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2" if M < 50 else "1"
    51→    os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"
    52→    
    53→    return aiter.fused_moe.fused_moe(
    54→        hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
    55→        topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
    56→        activation=aiter.ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=aiter.QuantType.per_1x32,
    57→        doweight_stage1=False,
    58→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
    59→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    60→        hidden_pad=config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"],
    61→        intermediate_pad=config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"],
    62→    )
    63→
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Competing team analysis reveals critical finding**: Most "winning" submissions from OpenCode Infinity and Gemini squad are experimental dead ends — NOT actual ranked leaders:
- Infinity's "winning" MoE falls back to `ref_kernel(data)` (stub!)
- Infinity's GEMM uses `tl.dot_scaled` Triton (we proved 1.34x slower than CK ASM)
- Gemini v19 MLA uses custom Triton flash-decode (we proved 9-127x slower)
- Gemini v12 GEMM uses fused Triton `tl.dot_scaled` (same dead end)

**The real top-10 competitors aren't in these directories.** They're using the same aiter primitives we are, just with better parameter tuning or routing.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me now look at the Gemini team's fused HIP GEMM and their MoE ultimate — those might have actually useful techniques.

```
     1→import torch
     2→import os
     3→import sys
     4→import ctypes
     5→from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline
     6→from task import input_t, output_t
     7→from aiter import dtypes

     9→
    10→# ULTIMATE FUSED HIP GEMM V2
    11→# Fixed primitive type mismatches for MI355X (gfx950).
    12→# Targeted for Rank #1 (9.7us).
    13→
    14→HIP_SRC = r'''
    15→#include <hip/hip_runtime.h>
    16→#include <hip/hip_bf16.h>
    17→#include <cstdint>
    18→
    19→#define BLOCK 256
    20→
    21→__device__ __forceinline__ float bf16_to_fp32(__hip_bfloat16 val) {
    22→    union {
    23→        uint32_t u32;
    24→        float f32;
    25→    } u;
    26→    u.u32 = (uint32_t)reinterpret_cast<uint16_t&>(val) << 16;
    27→    return u.f32;
    28→}
    29→
    30→__device__ __forceinline__ uint32_t pack_8x4bit(const uint8_t* nibbles) {
    31→    uint32_t packed = 0;
    32→    #pragma unroll
    33→    for (int i = 0; i < 8; i++) {
    34→        packed |= (static_cast<uint32_t>(nibbles[i] & 0x0F) << (i * 4));
    35→    }
    36→    return packed;
    37→}
    38→
    39→__device__ __forceinline__ uint8_t quantize_bf16_to_mxfp4(__hip_bfloat16 val, uint8_t exp_biased) {
    40→    float f = bf16_to_fp32(val);
    41→    unsigned int u32 = __float_as_uint(f);
    42→    unsigned int sign = u32 & 0x80000000u;
    43→    u32 ^= sign;
    44→    float f_pos = __uint_as_float(u32);
    45→    
    46→    float quant_scale = exp2f((float)(129 - exp_biased));
    47→    float qx_pos = f_pos * quant_scale;
    48→    
    49→    unsigned char e2m1;
    50→    if (qx_pos >= 6.0f) {
    51→        e2m1 = 0x7u;
    52→    } else if (qx_pos < 1.0f) {
    53→        float denormal_x = qx_pos + __uint_as_float(0x4A800000u);
    54→        unsigned int du = __float_as_uint(denormal_x) - 0x4A800000u;
    55→        e2m1 = (unsigned char)du;
    56→    } else {
    57→        unsigned int qx = __float_as_uint(qx_pos);
    58→        unsigned int mant_odd = (qx >> 22) & 1u;
    59→        qx += 0xC11FFFFFu;
    60→        qx += mant_odd;
    61→        qx >>= 22;
    62→        e2m1 = (unsigned char)qx;
    63→    }
    64→    e2m1 |= (unsigned char)(sign >> 28);
    65→    return e2m1;
    66→}
    67→
    68→__device__ __forceinline__ int shuffle_index(int row, int grp, int sm, int sn) {
    69→    int d0 = row >> 5;
    70→    int r32 = row & 31;
    71→    int d1 = r32 >> 4;
    72→    int d2 = r32 & 15;
    73→    int d3 = grp >> 3;
    74→    int c8 = grp & 7;
    75→    int d4 = c8 >> 2;
    76→    int d5 = c8 & 3;
    77→    int stride_d0 = (sn >> 3) * 256;
    78→    return d0 * stride_d0 + d3 * 256 + d5 * 64 + d2 * 4 + d4 * 2 + d1;
    79→}
    80→
    81→__global__ void fused_quant_shuffle_kernel(
    82→    const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ A,
    83→    unsigned char* __restrict__ A_q,
    84→    unsigned char* __restrict__ A_scale_shuffled,
    85→    int M, int K, int sm, int sn) 
    86→{
    87→    int global_block_idx = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;
    88→    int num_groups_per_row = K / 32;
    89→    int row = global_block_idx / num_groups_per_row;
    90→    int grp = global_block_idx % num_groups_per_row;
    91→    
    92→    if (row >= M || grp >= num_groups_per_row) return;
    93→
    94→    int base_idx = row * K + grp * 32;
    95→    const uint4* A_vec = reinterpret_cast<const uint4*>(&A[base_idx]);
    96→    
    97→    __hip_bfloat16 local_A[32];
    98→    #pragma unroll
    99→    for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
   100→        reinterpret_cast<uint4*>(local_A)[i] = A_vec[i];
   101→    }
   102→
   103→    float group_max = 0.0f;
   104→    #pragma unroll
   105→    for (int i = 0; i < 32; i++) {
   106→        float f = fabsf(bf16_to_fp32(local_A[i]));
   107→        if (f > group_max) group_max = f;
   108→    }
   109→
   110→    unsigned int u32_max = __float_as_uint(group_max);
   111→    unsigned int rounded = (u32_max + 0x200000u) & 0xFF800000u;
   112→    int exp_biased = (int)((rounded >> 23) & 0xFFu);
   113→    uint8_t scale_byte = (uint8_t)(exp_biased - 2);
   114→
   115→    uint8_t q_nibbles[32];
   116→    #pragma unroll
   117→    for (int i = 0; i < 32; i++) {
   118→        q_nibbles[i] = quantize_bf16_to_mxfp4(local_A[i], (uint8_t)exp_biased);
   119→    }
   120→
   121→    uint4 packed_out;
   122→    packed_out.x = pack_8x4bit(&q_nibbles[0]);
   123→    packed_out.y = pack_8x4bit(&q_nibbles[8]);
   124→    packed_out.z = pack_8x4bit(&q_nibbles[16]);
   125→    packed_out.w = pack_8x4bit(&q_nibbles[24]);
   126→
   127→    int out_base_idx = (row * (K / 2) + grp * 16) / 16; 
   128→    reinterpret_cast<uint4*>(A_q)[out_base_idx] = packed_out;
   129→
   130→    A_scale_shuffled[shuffle_index(row, grp, sm, sn)] = scale_byte;
   131→}
   132→
   133→void launch_fused_quant_shuffle(
   134→    torch::Tensor A, torch::Tensor A_q, torch::Tensor A_scale_sh,
   135→    int M, int K, int sm, int sn
   136→) {
   137→    int num_groups = M * (K / 32);
   138→    int blocks = (num_groups + BLOCK - 1) / BLOCK;
   139→    hipLaunchKernelGGL(fused_quant_shuffle_kernel, dim3(blocks), dim3(BLOCK), 0, 0,
   140→        (const __hip_bfloat16*)A.data_ptr(),
   141→        (unsigned char*)A_q.data_ptr(),
   142→        (unsigned char*)A_scale_sh.data_ptr(),
   143→        M, K, sm, sn);
   144→}
   145→'''
   146→
   147→_MOD = None
   148→_BUF_CACHE = {}
   149→
   150→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   151→    global _MOD
   152→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
   153→    M, K = A.shape
   154→    N = B.shape[0]
   155→
   156→    if _MOD is None:
   157→        try:
   158→            _MOD = load_inline(
   159→                name="fused_hip_v2_ext",
   160→                cpp_sources=["void launch_fused_quant_shuffle(torch::Tensor A, torch::Tensor A_q, torch::Tensor A_scale_sh, int M, int K, int sm, int sn);"],
   161→                cuda_sources=[HIP_SRC],
   162→                functions=["launch_fused_quant_shuffle"],
   163→                with_cuda=True,
   164→                extra_cflags=["-O3"],
   165→                verbose=False
   166→            )
   167→        except Exception as e:
   168→            print(f"HIP Build FAILED: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   169→            from reference import ref_kernel
   170→            return ref_kernel(data)
   171→
   172→    key = (M, K, N)
   173→    if key not in _BUF_CACHE:
   174→        sm = ((M + 255) // 256) * 256
   175→        sn = ((K // 32 + 7) // 8) * 8
   176→        A_q = torch.empty((M, K // 2), dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda")
   177→        A_scale_sh = torch.zeros(sm * sn, dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda")
   178→        out = torch.empty((M, N), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   179→        _BUF_CACHE[key] = (A_q, A_scale_sh, out, sm, sn)
   180→    
   181→    A_q, A_scale_sh, out, sm, sn = _BUF_CACHE[key]
   182→
   183→    # 1. Fused Quant + Shuffle (Correct primitive types)
   184→    _MOD.launch_fused_quant_shuffle(A.contiguous(), A_q, A_scale_sh, M, K, sm, sn)
   185→
   186→    # 2. ASM Dispatch
   187→    kn = "REDACTED" if M <= 64 else "REDACTED"
   188→    
   189→    log2_ks = None
   190→    if K >= 4096: log2_ks = 3
   191→    elif K >= 2048: log2_ks = 2
   192→    elif K >= 1024: log2_ks = 1
   193→
   194→    return aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm(
   195→        A_q.view(dtypes.fp4x2),
   196→        B_shuffle,
   197→        A_scale_sh.view(sm, sn).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0),
   198→        B_scale_sh,
   199→        out,
   200→        kn,
   201→        bpreshuffle=True,
   202→        log2_k_split=log2_ks
   203→    )
   204→
```

```
     1→import torch
     2→import os
     3→import aiter
     4→from task import input_t, output_t
     5→from reference import ref_kernel
     6→from aiter.fused_moe import moe_sorting, get_inter_dim
     7→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
     8→
     9→# ULTIMATE MOE V7: DIRECT CK-TILE DISPATCH
    10→# Bypasses Python wrapper overhead (~50us) to hit Rank #1.
    11→
    12→CACHE = {}
    13→
    14→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    15→    (
    16→        hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
    17→        gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
    18→        gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
    19→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    20→        topk_weights, topk_ids, config
    21→    ) = data
    22→
    23→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
    24→    E = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
    25→    DH = config["d_hidden"]
    26→    
    27→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
    28→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
    29→    topk = topk_ids.shape[1]
    30→
    31→    key = (M, E, DH)
    32→    if key not in CACHE:
    33→        # Pre-allocate routing buffers
    34→        # aiter sorting logic needs these
    35→        num_tokens = M * topk
    36→        sorted_ids = torch.empty(num_tokens, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    37→        sorted_weights = torch.empty(num_tokens, dtype=torch.float32, device="cuda")
    38→        sorted_expert_ids = torch.empty(E, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    39→        num_valid_ids = torch.empty(E, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    40→        moe_buf = torch.empty((M, DH), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
    41→        
    42→        # Intermediates for 2-stage CK
    43→        # Stage 1 out is [M*topk, inter_pad*2]
    44→        inter_pad = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[1] // 2
    45→        inter_out = torch.empty((M * topk, inter_pad * 2), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
    46→        
    47→        CACHE[key] = {
    48→            "sorted_ids": sorted_ids,
    49→            "sorted_weights": sorted_weights,
    50→            "sorted_expert_ids": sorted_expert_ids,
    51→            "num_valid_ids": num_valid_ids,
    52→            "moe_buf": moe_buf,
    53→            "inter_out": inter_out
    54→        }
    55→
    56→    c = CACHE[key]
    57→
    58→    # 1. Direct Sorting Call
    59→    torch.ops.aiter.moe_sorting_fwd(
    60→        topk_ids, topk_weights,
    61→        c["sorted_ids"], c["sorted_weights"],
    62→        c["sorted_expert_ids"], c["num_valid_ids"],
    63→        c["moe_buf"], E, DH, None, None, 0
    64→    )
    65→
    66→    # 2. Stage 1: Gate + Up GEMM
    67→    # Signature from probe: cktile_moe_gemm1(XQ, WQ, Y, sorted_ids, sorted_expert_ids, num_valid_ids, topk, ...)
    68→    torch.ops.aiter.cktile_moe_gemm1(
    69→        hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, c["inter_out"],
    70→        c["sorted_ids"], c["sorted_expert_ids"], c["num_valid_ids"],
    71→        topk, intermediate_pad, hidden_pad,
    72→        c["sorted_weights"], None, gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, None,
    73→        int(ActivationType.Silu), 32, 1
    74→    )
    75→
    76→    # 3. Stage 2: Down GEMM
    77→    # Stage 2 writes directly to the final output buffer
    78→    final_out = torch.zeros((M, DH), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
    79→    
    80→    torch.ops.aiter.cktile_moe_gemm2(
    81→        c["inter_out"], down_weight_shuffled, final_out,
    82→        c["sorted_ids"], c["sorted_expert_ids"], c["num_valid_ids"],
    83→        topk, hidden_pad, intermediate_pad,
    84→        c["sorted_weights"], None, down_weight_scale_shuffled, None,
    85→        int(ActivationType.No), 32, 1
    86→    )
    87→
    88→    return final_out
    89→
```

```
    50→def _select_block_m_optimized(num_tokens, topk, num_experts, inter_dim, d_hidden):
    51→    """Optimized block_m selection with shape-specific tuning."""
    52→    tile_n = 128
    53→    tg_n = (inter_dim + tile_n - 1) // tile_n
    54→
    55→    # Shape-specific tuning based on benchmark analysis

    68→        else:

    77→        score = (rounds, empty, bm)
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Key findings from competitor analysis:**

1. **MoE Direct CK Dispatch** (Infinity `submission_optimized.py`): The most promising technique. Bypasses `fused_moe()` Python wrapper by calling `aiter.moe_sorting_fwd` → `fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm1` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm2` directly. Includes CU-aware `block_m` selection. Potential 3-8µs savings.

2. **MLA Routing Bug in Our Code**: Our matmul condition uses `OR` logic (`bs <= 4 OR total_kv <= 32768`), sending large-KV shapes to the slow bf16 matmul path. Infinity uses `AND` logic — only matmul when batch is SMALL **and** KV is MODERATE. For bs=4, kvseqlen=131072, our OR sends it to bf16 matmul (600MB reads) while aiter fp8 path reads only 300MB.

3. **GEMM at CK ASM ceiling**: Both competing teams use identical primitives (HIP quant + `gemm_a4w4_asm`). The same tile configs and split-K values. Our approach is already at the kernel-level performance ceiling.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The OR→AND routing fix is the most impactful change:**
- With `OR`: bs=2, kvseqlen=131072 → total_kv=262K → bf16 matmul reads 262K×576×2=302MB
- With `AND`: same shape → aiter fp8 reads 262K×576×1=151MB (2x less bandwidth!)
- MI355X has ~3.2 TB/s HBM bandwidth, so 151MB savings = ~47µs reduction on that shape
- The A16W8 extension (262K→524K) also eliminates 3 kernel launches for Q quantization on medium shapes

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**MoE Direct CK Dispatch architecture:**
- `fused_moe()` does: Python routing → sort → quant → GEMM1 → activation → GEMM2 → reduce
- Direct dispatch: Pre-allocated buffers + `moe_sorting_fwd` → `fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm1` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm2`
- Key savings: No Python-level buffer allocation per call, no type checking, no dispatch overhead
- CU-aware `block_m` selection ensures tile grids maximize MI355X compute unit utilization
- Fallback to proven `fused_moe()` if ANY direct dispatch function is missing
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Now let me also apply split-K improvements to the **GEMM** for the M≤16 shapes. Let me re-read the current GEMM to apply the Gemini pattern of always using `192x128` for M≤256 (instead of our current M≤128→32x128, M≤256→192x128):

```
   232→def _kernel_name(tile_m, tile_n):
   233→    sym = f"f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_{tile_m}x{tile_n}"

   237→def _get_config(M, N, K):
   238→    """
   239→    Improved configuration selection with adaptive tile sizing.
   240→    Uses mathematical relationships to determine optimal tile sizes
   241→    rather than relying solely on hardcoded thresholds.
   242→    """
   243→    key = (M, N, K)
   244→    if key not in _config_cache:
   245→        kernel_name = None
   246→        split_k = None
   247→        csv_found = False
   248→        try:
   249→            cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
   250→            if isinstance(cfg, dict):
   251→                csv_found = True
   252→                kn = cfg.get("kernelName")
   253→                if kn is not None:
   254→                    kernel_name = str(kn)
   255→                sk = cfg.get("splitK")
   256→                if sk is not None and int(sk) > 0:
   257→                    split_k = int(sk)
   258→            elif cfg is not None:
   259→                csv_found = True
   260→                kernel_name = str(cfg)
   261→        except Exception:
   262→            pass
   263→
   264→        # Fallback: use known-valid tile configs from runner's .co files
   265→        # Probe confirmed: 32x128, 64x256, 192x128 available on runner
   266→        # CSV prefers 32x128 for M=64 and M=256 — use it as default
   267→        if kernel_name is None:
   268→            if M <= 128:
   269→                kernel_name = _kernel_name(32, 128)
   270→            elif M <= 256:
   271→                kernel_name = _kernel_name(192, 128)
   272→            else:
   273→                kernel_name = _kernel_name(64, 256)
   274→
   275→        # Split-K for memory-bound shapes (small M, large K)
   276→        # Force override regardless of CSV splitK value
   277→        if M <= 64 and K >= 1024:
   278→            if K >= 4096:
   279→                split_k = 3  # 8-way split
   280→            elif K >= 2048:
   281→                split_k = 2  # 4-way split
   282→            else:
   283→                split_k = 1  # 2-way split
   284→
   285→        _config_cache[key] = (kernel_name, split_k)
```

Let me compute the geomeans and analyze the results.

**MLA Benchmark** (median µs):
| Shape | Time |
|-------|------|
| bs=4, kv=1K | 23.7 |
| bs=4, kv=8K | 37.0 |
| bs=32, kv=1K | 39.6 |
| bs=32, kv=8K | 94.9 |
| bs=64, kv=1K | 42.0 |
| bs=64, kv=8K | 170 |
| bs=256, kv=1K | 89.0 |
| bs=256, kv=8K | 303 |

Geomean ≈ 70.9µs (vs 68.7µs previous = **3% regression**)

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The AND logic was wrong!** The OR logic was correct all along. Here's why:
- With OR: `bs=32, kv=1024` → `total_kv=32768 ≤ 32768` → **matmul** (fast, L2-cached)
- With AND: `bs=32, kv=1024` → `bs=32 > 8` → **aiter** (slower for small KV due to dispatch overhead)
- Small bs AND small total_kv are INDEPENDENT reasons to prefer matmul, hence OR is correct
- The A16W8 extension (262K→524K) still helps by skipping Q quantization for `bs=64, kv=8192`
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me fix MLA — revert to OR logic, keep A16W8 extension and split cap:
