  "content": "\"\"\"\nMXFP4 MoE: Simple four-bucket KSPLIT routing with module-level env vars.\nRestored from sprint/moe_ksplit6_257e.py pattern — no torch.bincount, no sparsity calc.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nimport torch\nfrom task import input_t, output_t\nfrom aiter import ActivationType, QuantType\nfrom aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe\n\n# Module-level: enable non-temporal loads + optimized sorting before any kernel call\nos.environ[\"AITER_USE_NT\"] = \"1\"\nos.environ[\"AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING\"] = \"1\"\n\n_state: dict = {\"ksplit\": None, \"block_m\": None}\n\n\ndef custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:\n    (\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight,\n        down_weight,\n        gate_up_weight_scale,\n        down_weight_scale,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        config,\n    ) = data\n\n    hidden_pad = config[\"d_hidden_pad\"] - config[\"d_hidden\"]\n    intermediate_pad = config[\"d_expert_pad\"] - config[\"d_expert\"]\n\n    num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]\n    estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts\n    is_large_expert = num_experts >= 200\n\n    # Routing — KSPLIT=2 max (HARD: ≥3 causes overflow ~1e35)\n    # Large-expert shapes (257E) use default when est_m >= 16 (tuned config is better)\n    if estimated_m >= 80:\n        ks = \"default\"\n        bm = None\n    elif is_large_expert and estimated_m >= 16:\n        ks = \"default\"\n        bm = None\n    elif estimated_m >= 25:\n        ks = \"2\"\n        bm = None\n    elif estimated_m >= 8:\n        ks = \"2\"\n        bm = \"64\"\n    else:\n        ks = \"2\"\n        bm = \"64\"\n\n    # Update env vars only when bucket changes\n    if _state[\"ksplit\"] != ks or _state[\"block_m\"] != bm:\n        if ks == \"default\":\n            os.environ.pop(\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\", None)\n            os.environ.pop(\"AITER_KSPLIT\", None)\n            os.environ.pop(\"AITER_BLOCK_M\", None)\n        else:\n            os.environ[\"AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG\"] = \"1\"\n            os.environ[\"AITER_KSPLIT\"] = ks\n            if bm:\n                os.environ[\"AITER_BLOCK_M\"] = bm\n            else:\n                os.environ.pop(\"AITER_BLOCK_M\", None)\n        _state[\"ksplit\"] = ks\n        _state[\"block_m\"] = bm\n\n    return fused_moe(\n        hidden_states,\n        gate_up_weight_shuffled,\n        down_weight_shuffled,\n        topk_weights,\n        topk_ids,\n        expert_mask=None,\n        activation=ActivationType.Silu,\n        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,\n        doweight_stage1=False,\n        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,\n        a1_scale=None,\n        a2_scale=None,\n        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,\n        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,\n    )\n"
}

     1→"""
     2→MLA decode: three-regime routing with adaptive KV splits and metadata caching.
     3→Improved cache efficiency with reduced metadata computation overhead.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→import torch
     7→from task import input_t, output_t
     8→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
     9→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    10→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    11→
    12→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576**0.5)
    13→V_HEAD_DIM = 512
    14→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    15→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    16→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    17→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    18→BF16_DTYPE = torch.bfloat16
    19→
    20→# ─── Regime thresholds (Phase 2 — always A16W8, skip Q quantization) ────
    21→MATMUL_MAX_BS = 4
    22→MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 32768
    23→
    24→# Cache for metadata computation results
    25→_cache: dict = {}
    26→# Pre-allocated output buffers keyed on (total_q, nheads)
    27→_out_buf: dict = {}
    28→# Cache for num_kv_splits computations to avoid repeated calculations
    29→_splits_cache: dict = {}
    30→
    31→
    32→def _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:
    33→    """Adaptive split-K factor — capped at 16 (32 adds reduction overhead)."""
    34→    if total_kv not in _splits_cache:
    35→        if total_kv <= 4096:
    36→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 1
    37→        elif total_kv <= 32768:
    38→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 4
    39→        elif total_kv <= 131072:
    40→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 8
    41→        else:
    42→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 16
    43→    return _splits_cache[total_kv]
    44→
    45→
    46→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    47→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    48→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    49→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    50→    return (
    51→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    52→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    53→    )
    54→
    55→
    56→def _build_cache(
    57→    bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype, num_kv_splits, qo_indptr, kv_indptr
    58→):
    59→    """
    60→    Build metadata cache with optimizations.
    61→    Precompute values that don't change across calls to reduce computation.
    62→    """
    63→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    64→
    65→    # Precompute kv_indices and kv_last_page_len outside the metadata function
    66→    # since they only depend on kv_indptr which changes less frequently
    67→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    68→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
    69→
    70→    # Compute metadata info once
    71→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    72→        bs,
    73→        qseqlen,
    74→        nheads,
    75→        q_dtype,
    76→        kv_dtype,
    77→        is_sparse=False,
    78→        fast_mode=True,
    79→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits,
    80→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    81→    )
    82→
    83→    # Allocate work buffers
    84→    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
    85→
    86→    # Populate metadata buffers
    87→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    88→        qo_indptr,
    89→        kv_indptr,
    90→        kv_last_page_len,
    91→        nheads // NUM_KV_HEADS,
    92→        NUM_KV_HEADS,
    93→        True,
    94→        wm,
    95→        wis,
    96→        wi,
    97→        ri,
    98→        rfm,
    99→        rpm,
   100→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   101→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   102→        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
   103→        uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
   104→        fast_mode=True,
   105→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   106→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   107→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   108→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   109→    )
   110→
   111→    return {
   112→        "kv_indices": kv_indices,
   113→        "kv_last_page_len": kv_last_page_len,
   114→        "work_meta_data": wm,
   115→        "work_indptr": wi,
   116→        "work_info_set": wis,
   117→        "reduce_indptr": ri,
   118→        "reduce_final_map": rfm,
   119→        "reduce_partial_map": rpm,
   120→        "num_kv_splits": num_kv_splits,
   121→    }
   122→
   123→
   124→def _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads):
   125→    """Regime 1: 3D batched matmul — bypasses aiter pipeline entirely."""
   126→    kv = kv_data["bf16"].view(bs, kvseqlen, QK_HEAD_DIM)
   127→
   128→    # True 3D batched GEMM: no broadcast, no string parsing
   129→    q_3d = q.view(bs, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM)  # [bs, nheads, 576]
   130→    kv_t = kv.transpose(1, 2)  # [bs, 576, kvseqlen]
   131→    scores = torch.matmul(q_3d, kv_t).mul_(SM_SCALE)  # [bs, nheads, kvseqlen]
   132→    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   133→
   134→    v = kv[:, :, :V_HEAD_DIM]  # [bs, kvseqlen, 512]
   135→    out = torch.matmul(weights, v)  # [bs, nheads, 512]
   136→
   137→    # Reshape to match expected output: [total_q, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM]
   138→    return out.unsqueeze(1).reshape(-1, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM)
   139→
   140→
   141→def _aiter_decode(
   142→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv, use_a16w8
   143→):
   144→    """Regime 2/3: aiter mla_decode_fwd with metadata caching."""
   145→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   146→    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])
   147→
   148→    if use_a16w8:
   149→        q_input = q
   150→        q_scale = None
   151→        q_dtype = BF16_DTYPE
   152→    else:
   153→        q_input, q_scale = _quantize_fp8(q)
   154→        q_dtype = FP8_DTYPE
   155→
   156→    num_kv_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)
   157→    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, use_a16w8, num_kv_splits)
   158→
   159→    # Optimize cache lookup by prefetching commonly accessed attributes
   160→    if key not in _cache:
   161→        _cache[key] = _build_cache(
   162→            bs,
   163→            qseqlen,
   164→            kvseqlen,
   165→            nheads,
   166→            q_dtype,
   167→            FP8_DTYPE,
   168→            num_kv_splits,
   169→            qo_indptr,
   170→            kv_indptr,
   171→        )
   172→    c = _cache[key]
   173→
   174→    out_key = (q.shape[0], nheads)
   175→    if out_key not in _out_buf:
   176→        _out_buf[out_key] = torch.empty(
   177→            (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),
   178→            dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   179→            device="cuda",
   180→        )
   181→    o = _out_buf[out_key]
   182→
   183→    mla_decode_fwd(
   184→        q_input.view(-1, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   185→        kv_4d,
   186→        o,
   187→        qo_indptr,
   188→        kv_indptr,
   189→        c["kv_indices"],
   190→        c["kv_last_page_len"],
   191→        qseqlen,
   192→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   193→        nhead_kv=NUM_KV_HEADS,
   194→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   195→        logit_cap=0.0,
   196→        num_kv_splits=c["num_kv_splits"],
   197→        q_scale=q_scale,
   198→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   199→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   200→        work_meta_data=c["work_meta_data"],
   201→        work_indptr=c["work_indptr"],
   202→        work_info_set=c["work_info_set"],
   203→        reduce_indptr=c["reduce_indptr"],
   204→        reduce_final_map=c["reduce_final_map"],
   205→        reduce_partial_map=c["reduce_partial_map"],
   206→    )
   207→    return o
   208→
   209→
   210→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   211→    """
   212→    MLA decode kernel with improved cache efficiency.
   213→    """
   214→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   215→    bs = config["batch_size"]
   216→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
   217→    qseqlen = config["q_seq_len"]
   218→    nheads = config["num_heads"]
   219→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
   220→
   221→    # Regime 1: 3D matmul for small batches OR small KV (bypass aiter overhead)
   222→    # OR is correct: small bs OR small total_kv are independent reasons to use matmul
   223→    if bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS or total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV:
   224→        return _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads)
   225→
   226→    # Regime 2: a16w8 for medium (skip Q quantization)
   227→    # Regime 3: a8w8 for large (fp8 Q halves bandwidth)
   228→    use_a16w8 = total_kv <= A16W8_THRESHOLD
   229→    return _aiter_decode(
   230→        q,
   231→        kv_data,
   232→        qo_indptr,
   233→        kv_indptr,
   234→        config,
   235→        bs,
   236→        kvseqlen,
   237→        qseqlen,
   238→        nheads,
   239→        total_kv,
   240→        use_a16w8,
   241→    )
   242→
```

> AGENT

Confirmed — line 228 references `A16W8_THRESHOLD` which no longer exists. Now implementing the "always A16W8" approach: always pass bf16 Q directly to `mla_decode_fwd`, eliminating 3 kernel launches (amax, div, clamp+cast) for quantization.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why "always A16W8" should win**: In MLA decode, Q tensor is tiny (bs × nheads × 576) vs KV cache (bs × kvseqlen × 576). Even at the largest benchmark shape (bs=256, kv=8192), Q is 4.7MB bf16 vs 1.2GB KV fp8. The fp8 Q bandwidth saving (~0.75µs) is dwarfed by the 3 quantization kernel launches (~3µs). So A16W8 mode (pass bf16 Q directly) should always be faster.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

     1→"""
     2→MLA decode: two-regime routing — always A16W8 (skip Q quantization entirely).
     3→Q tensor is tiny vs KV cache; 3 quantization kernels cost more than fp8 bandwidth saves.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→import torch
     7→from task import input_t, output_t
     8→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
     9→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    10→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    11→
    12→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576**0.5)
    13→V_HEAD_DIM = 512
    14→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    15→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    16→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    17→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    18→BF16_DTYPE = torch.bfloat16
    19→
    20→# ─── Regime thresholds (Phase 2 — always A16W8, skip Q quantization) ────
    21→MATMUL_MAX_BS = 4
    22→MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 32768
    23→
    24→# Cache for metadata computation results
    25→_cache: dict = {}
    26→# Pre-allocated output buffers keyed on (total_q, nheads)
    27→_out_buf: dict = {}
    28→# Cache for num_kv_splits computations to avoid repeated calculations
    29→_splits_cache: dict = {}
    30→
    31→
    32→def _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:
    33→    """Adaptive split-K factor — capped at 16 (32 adds reduction overhead)."""
    34→    if total_kv not in _splits_cache:
    35→        if total_kv <= 4096:
    36→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 1
    37→        elif total_kv <= 32768:
    38→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 4
    39→        elif total_kv <= 131072:
    40→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 8
    41→        else:
    42→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 16
    43→    return _splits_cache[total_kv]
    44→
    45→
    46→
    47→def _build_cache(
    48→    bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype, num_kv_splits, qo_indptr, kv_indptr
    49→):
    50→    """
    51→    Build metadata cache with optimizations.
    52→    Precompute values that don't change across calls to reduce computation.
    53→    """
    54→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    55→
    56→    # Precompute kv_indices and kv_last_page_len outside the metadata function
    57→    # since they only depend on kv_indptr which changes less frequently
    58→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    59→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
    60→
    61→    # Compute metadata info once
    62→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    63→        bs,
    64→        qseqlen,
    65→        nheads,
    66→        q_dtype,
    67→        kv_dtype,
    68→        is_sparse=False,
    69→        fast_mode=True,
    70→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits,
    71→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    72→    )
    73→
    74→    # Allocate work buffers
    75→    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
    76→
    77→    # Populate metadata buffers
    78→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    79→        qo_indptr,
    80→        kv_indptr,
    81→        kv_last_page_len,
    82→        nheads // NUM_KV_HEADS,
    83→        NUM_KV_HEADS,
    84→        True,
    85→        wm,
    86→        wis,
    87→        wi,
    88→        ri,
    89→        rfm,
    90→        rpm,
    91→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
    92→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
    93→        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
    94→        uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
    95→        fast_mode=True,
    96→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
    97→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    98→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
    99→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   100→    )
   101→
   102→    return {
   103→        "kv_indices": kv_indices,
   104→        "kv_last_page_len": kv_last_page_len,
   105→        "work_meta_data": wm,
   106→        "work_indptr": wi,
   107→        "work_info_set": wis,
   108→        "reduce_indptr": ri,
   109→        "reduce_final_map": rfm,
   110→        "reduce_partial_map": rpm,
   111→        "num_kv_splits": num_kv_splits,
   112→    }
   113→
   114→
   115→def _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads):
   116→    """Regime 1: 3D batched matmul — bypasses aiter pipeline entirely."""
   117→    kv = kv_data["bf16"].view(bs, kvseqlen, QK_HEAD_DIM)
   118→
   119→    # True 3D batched GEMM: no broadcast, no string parsing
   120→    q_3d = q.view(bs, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM)  # [bs, nheads, 576]
   121→    kv_t = kv.transpose(1, 2)  # [bs, 576, kvseqlen]
   122→    scores = torch.matmul(q_3d, kv_t).mul_(SM_SCALE)  # [bs, nheads, kvseqlen]
   123→    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   124→
   125→    v = kv[:, :, :V_HEAD_DIM]  # [bs, kvseqlen, 512]
   126→    out = torch.matmul(weights, v)  # [bs, nheads, 512]
   127→
   128→    # Reshape to match expected output: [total_q, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM]
   129→    return out.unsqueeze(1).reshape(-1, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM)
   130→
   131→
   132→def _aiter_decode(
   133→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv
   134→):
   135→    """Regime 2: always A16W8 — pass bf16 Q directly, skip quantization."""
   136→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   137→    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])
   138→
   139→    num_kv_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)
   140→    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, num_kv_splits)
   141→
   142→    # Optimize cache lookup by prefetching commonly accessed attributes
   143→    if key not in _cache:
   144→        _cache[key] = _build_cache(
   145→            bs,
   146→            qseqlen,
   147→            kvseqlen,
   148→            nheads,
   149→            BF16_DTYPE,
   150→            FP8_DTYPE,
   151→            num_kv_splits,
   152→            qo_indptr,
   153→            kv_indptr,
   154→        )
   155→    c = _cache[key]
   156→
   157→    out_key = (q.shape[0], nheads)
   158→    if out_key not in _out_buf:
   159→        _out_buf[out_key] = torch.empty(
   160→            (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),
   161→            dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   162→            device="cuda",
   163→        )
   164→    o = _out_buf[out_key]
   165→
   166→    mla_decode_fwd(
   167→        q.view(-1, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   168→        kv_4d,
   169→        o,
   170→        qo_indptr,
   171→        kv_indptr,
   172→        c["kv_indices"],
   173→        c["kv_last_page_len"],
   174→        qseqlen,
   175→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   176→        nhead_kv=NUM_KV_HEADS,
   177→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   178→        logit_cap=0.0,
   179→        num_kv_splits=c["num_kv_splits"],
   180→        q_scale=None,
   181→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   182→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   183→        work_meta_data=c["work_meta_data"],
   184→        work_indptr=c["work_indptr"],
   185→        work_info_set=c["work_info_set"],
   186→        reduce_indptr=c["reduce_indptr"],
   187→        reduce_final_map=c["reduce_final_map"],
   188→        reduce_partial_map=c["reduce_partial_map"],
   189→    )
   190→    return o
   191→
   192→
   193→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   194→    """
   195→    MLA decode kernel with improved cache efficiency.
   196→    """
   197→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   198→    bs = config["batch_size"]
   199→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
   200→    qseqlen = config["q_seq_len"]
   201→    nheads = config["num_heads"]
   202→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
   203→
   204→    # Regime 1: 3D matmul for small batches OR small KV (bypass aiter overhead)
   205→    # OR is correct: small bs OR small total_kv are independent reasons to use matmul
   206→    if bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS or total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV:
   207→        return _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads)
   208→
   209→    # Regime 2: always A16W8 — Q is tiny, quantization overhead > bandwidth saving
   210→    return _aiter_decode(
   211→        q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv
   212→    )
   213→
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Always-A16W8 REGRESSED by +4.6µs (76.4 vs 71.8)**. The hypothesis was wrong — for the largest shapes (bs=256, kv=8192), the a16w8 ASM kernel (`mla_a16w8_qh16_m16x4_n16x1`) is actually **slower** than the a8w8 kernel (`mla_a8w8_qh16_qseqlen1_gqaratio16`). The a8w8 kernel has a different tile configuration optimized for large KV, and the 3-kernel quantization cost is recovered by the faster ASM compute. This is why the original code had a threshold.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The always-A16W8 approach is a dead end. I need to restore the A16W8 threshold. The original working values were `A16W8_THRESHOLD = 262144`. Let me restore the two-regime approach:

  "new_string": "\"\"\"\nMLA decode: three-regime routing with adaptive KV splits and metadata caching.\nRegime 1: matmul (small bs/KV), Regime 2: A16W8, Regime 3: A8W8 (large KV).\n\"\"\""
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py",
  "old_string": "def _aiter_decode(\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv\n):\n    \"\"\"Regime 2: always A16W8 — pass bf16 Q directly, skip quantization.\"\"\"\n    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data[\"fp8\"]\n    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])\n\n    num_kv_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)\n    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, num_kv_splits)",
  "new_string": "def _aiter_decode(\n    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv, use_a16w8\n):\n    \"\"\"Regime 2/3: aiter mla_decode_fwd with metadata caching.\"\"\"\n    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data[\"fp8\"]\n    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])\n\n    if use_a16w8:\n        q_input = q\n        q_scale = None\n        q_dtype = BF16_DTYPE\n    else:\n        q_input, q_scale = _quantize_fp8(q)\n        q_dtype = FP8_DTYPE\n\n    num_kv_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)\n    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, use_a16w8, num_kv_splits)"
}

     1→"""
     2→MLA decode: three-regime routing with adaptive KV splits and metadata caching.
     3→Regime 1: matmul (small bs/KV), Regime 2: A16W8, Regime 3: A8W8 (large KV).
     4→"""
     5→
     6→import torch
     7→from task import input_t, output_t
     8→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
     9→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    10→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    11→
    12→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (576**0.5)
    13→V_HEAD_DIM = 512
    14→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    15→QK_HEAD_DIM = 576
    16→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    17→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    18→BF16_DTYPE = torch.bfloat16
    19→
    20→# ─── Regime thresholds ────
    21→MATMUL_MAX_BS = 4
    22→MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 32768
    23→A16W8_THRESHOLD = 262144
    24→
    25→# Cache for metadata computation results
    26→_cache: dict = {}
    27→# Pre-allocated output buffers keyed on (total_q, nheads)
    28→_out_buf: dict = {}
    29→# Cache for num_kv_splits computations to avoid repeated calculations
    30→_splits_cache: dict = {}
    31→
    32→
    33→def _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:
    34→    """Adaptive split-K factor — capped at 16 (32 adds reduction overhead)."""
    35→    if total_kv not in _splits_cache:
    36→        if total_kv <= 4096:
    37→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 1
    38→        elif total_kv <= 32768:
    39→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 4
    40→        elif total_kv <= 131072:
    41→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 8
    42→        else:
    43→            _splits_cache[total_kv] = 16
    44→    return _splits_cache[total_kv]
    45→
    46→
    47→
    48→def _quantize_fp8(tensor):
    49→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    50→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    51→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    52→    return (
    53→        (tensor / scale).clamp(finfo.min, finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE),
    54→        scale.float().reshape(1),
    55→    )
    56→
    57→
    58→def _build_cache(
    59→    bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, q_dtype, kv_dtype, num_kv_splits, qo_indptr, kv_indptr
    60→):
    61→    """
    62→    Build metadata cache with optimizations.
    63→    Precompute values that don't change across calls to reduce computation.
    64→    """
    65→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
    66→
    67→    # Precompute kv_indices and kv_last_page_len outside the metadata function
    68→    # since they only depend on kv_indptr which changes less frequently
    69→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
    70→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
    71→
    72→    # Compute metadata info once
    73→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
    74→        bs,
    75→        qseqlen,
    76→        nheads,
    77→        q_dtype,
    78→        kv_dtype,
    79→        is_sparse=False,
    80→        fast_mode=True,
    81→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits,
    82→        intra_batch_mode=True,
    83→    )
    84→
    85→    # Allocate work buffers
    86→    wm, wi, wis, ri, rfm, rpm = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
    87→
    88→    # Populate metadata buffers
    89→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
    90→        qo_indptr,
    91→        kv_indptr,
    92→        kv_last_page_len,
    93→        nheads // NUM_KV_HEADS,
    94→        NUM_KV_HEADS,
    95→        True,
    96→        wm,
    97→        wis,
    98→        wi,
    99→        ri,
   100→        rfm,
   101→        rpm,
   102→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   103→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   104→        max_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
   105→        uni_seqlen_qo=qseqlen,
   106→        fast_mode=True,
   107→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   108→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   109→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   110→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   111→    )
   112→
   113→    return {
   114→        "kv_indices": kv_indices,
   115→        "kv_last_page_len": kv_last_page_len,
   116→        "work_meta_data": wm,
   117→        "work_indptr": wi,
   118→        "work_info_set": wis,
   119→        "reduce_indptr": ri,
   120→        "reduce_final_map": rfm,
   121→        "reduce_partial_map": rpm,
   122→        "num_kv_splits": num_kv_splits,
   123→    }
   124→
   125→
   126→def _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads):
   127→    """Regime 1: 3D batched matmul — bypasses aiter pipeline entirely."""
   128→    kv = kv_data["bf16"].view(bs, kvseqlen, QK_HEAD_DIM)
   129→
   130→    # True 3D batched GEMM: no broadcast, no string parsing
   131→    q_3d = q.view(bs, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM)  # [bs, nheads, 576]
   132→    kv_t = kv.transpose(1, 2)  # [bs, 576, kvseqlen]
   133→    scores = torch.matmul(q_3d, kv_t).mul_(SM_SCALE)  # [bs, nheads, kvseqlen]
   134→    weights = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   135→
   136→    v = kv[:, :, :V_HEAD_DIM]  # [bs, kvseqlen, 512]
   137→    out = torch.matmul(weights, v)  # [bs, nheads, 512]
   138→
   139→    # Reshape to match expected output: [total_q, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM]
   140→    return out.unsqueeze(1).reshape(-1, nheads, V_HEAD_DIM)
   141→
   142→
   143→def _aiter_decode(
   144→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv, use_a16w8
   145→):
   146→    """Regime 2/3: aiter mla_decode_fwd with metadata caching."""
   147→    kv_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   148→    kv_4d = kv_fp8.view(kv_fp8.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, NUM_KV_HEADS, kv_fp8.shape[-1])
   149→
   150→    if use_a16w8:
   151→        q_input = q
   152→        q_scale = None
   153→        q_dtype = BF16_DTYPE
   154→    else:
   155→        q_input, q_scale = _quantize_fp8(q)
   156→        q_dtype = FP8_DTYPE
   157→
   158→    num_kv_splits = _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv)
   159→    key = (bs, qseqlen, kvseqlen, nheads, use_a16w8, num_kv_splits)
   160→
   161→    # Optimize cache lookup by prefetching commonly accessed attributes
   162→    if key not in _cache:
   163→        _cache[key] = _build_cache(
   164→            bs,
   165→            qseqlen,
   166→            kvseqlen,
   167→            nheads,
   168→            q_dtype,
   169→            FP8_DTYPE,
   170→            num_kv_splits,
   171→            qo_indptr,
   172→            kv_indptr,
   173→        )
   174→    c = _cache[key]
   175→
   176→    out_key = (q.shape[0], nheads)
   177→    if out_key not in _out_buf:
   178→        _out_buf[out_key] = torch.empty(
   179→            (q.shape[0], nheads, V_HEAD_DIM),
   180→            dtype=torch.bfloat16,
   181→            device="cuda",
   182→        )
   183→    o = _out_buf[out_key]
   184→
   185→    mla_decode_fwd(
   186→        q_input.view(-1, nheads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   187→        kv_4d,
   188→        o,
   189→        qo_indptr,
   190→        kv_indptr,
   191→        c["kv_indices"],
   192→        c["kv_last_page_len"],
   193→        qseqlen,
   194→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   195→        nhead_kv=NUM_KV_HEADS,
   196→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   197→        logit_cap=0.0,
   198→        num_kv_splits=c["num_kv_splits"],
   199→        q_scale=q_scale,
   200→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   201→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   202→        work_meta_data=c["work_meta_data"],
   203→        work_indptr=c["work_indptr"],
   204→        work_info_set=c["work_info_set"],
   205→        reduce_indptr=c["reduce_indptr"],
   206→        reduce_final_map=c["reduce_final_map"],
   207→        reduce_partial_map=c["reduce_partial_map"],
   208→    )
   209→    return o
   210→
   211→
   212→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   213→    """
   214→    MLA decode kernel with improved cache efficiency.
   215→    """
   216→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   217→    bs = config["batch_size"]
   218→    kvseqlen = config["kv_seq_len"]
   219→    qseqlen = config["q_seq_len"]
   220→    nheads = config["num_heads"]
   221→    total_kv = bs * kvseqlen
   222→
   223→    # Regime 1: 3D matmul for small batches OR small KV (bypass aiter overhead)
   224→    # OR is correct: small bs OR small total_kv are independent reasons to use matmul
   225→    if bs <= MATMUL_MAX_BS or total_kv <= MATMUL_MAX_TOTAL_KV:
   226→        return _matmul_decode(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, nheads)
   227→
   228→    # Regime 2: A16W8 for medium (skip Q quantization)
   229→    # Regime 3: A8W8 for large (fp8 Q + faster ASM kernel)
   230→    use_a16w8 = total_kv <= A16W8_THRESHOLD
   231→    return _aiter_decode(
   232→        q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, bs, kvseqlen, qseqlen, nheads, total_kv, use_a16w8
   233→    )
   234→
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Critical finding**: The a16w8 ASM kernel (`mla_a16w8_qh16_m16x4_n16x1_coex0_mask1_ps`) is **~2x slower** than the a8w8 kernel at bs=256, kv=8192 — 591µs vs 302µs. This is NOT about Q quantization overhead. The a8w8 kernel binary has a completely different tile configuration (`qh16_qseqlen1_gqaratio16`) that's optimized for large batch decode. The a16w8 variant uses a small-batch tile (`m16x4_n16x1`) that doesn't scale. This is a **hardware-level kernel selection** issue, not a Python-level dispatch choice.

**Implication**: The A16W8_THRESHOLD is correctly placed at 262144 — shapes above this benefit enormously from the a8w8 kernel's tile config, more than offsetting Q quantization cost.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Baseline confirmed at 70.1µs geomean. Now let me think about what's left to try for MLA. The MLA bottleneck is clearly the ASM kernel itself. Let me check what the "competitive ceiling" skill says about this situation, then focus on areas where we can still make progress.

Given all the dead ends accumulated, let me assess the current state across all three kernels:

**Current standings:**
| Kernel | Current | Target | Gap | Status |
|--------|---------|--------|-----|--------|
| **MLA** | 70.1µs | ~55µs | 15µs | At ASM kernel ceiling — no Python-level gains possible |
| **MoE** | ~152µs | ~150µs | ~2µs | Restored baseline, env var probes untested |
| **GEMM** | ~23µs | ~12µs | ~11µs | Unchanged, HIP quant + CK ASM |

The MoE kernel has the closest gap. Let me submit a probe to discover what env vars the runner's `fused_moe` actually reads, then try any promising ones. But first, let me submit the MoE and GEMM to leaderboard since they're at their current best — we haven't done leaderboard submissions yet this sprint.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**MoE stderr is a goldmine**: The leaderboard run stderr reveals the actual fused_moe internal dispatch decisions:
- `ksplit = 2, block_m = 32` for small shapes (est_m=0,4)
- `ksplit = 2, block_m = 64` for medium (est_m=34)
- `ksplit = 0, block_m = 64` for large (est_m=139)  
- `ksplit = 0, block_m = 128` for very large (est_m=139, d_expert=2048)

The kernel actually uses `ksplit=0` (no split) for large shapes rather than our `"default"`, which means `fused_moe` is already choosing optimal configs for those cases. The `tuned_fmoe.csv` and model config CSVs are being loaded (`dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv`). Our env var overrides only matter for the small shapes.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

All three kernels are submitted to leaderboard. The remaining optimization opportunities are:

1. **MoE**: The stderr shows `fused_moe` is loading `dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv` — we could try `doweight_stage1=True` with a smaller shape (but it JIT-times out at 230s). The only env var trick left untested is `AITER_BLOCK_N` and `AITER_WAVES_PER_EU`.

2. **GEMM**: The `S2` shape (M=16, K=7168) has no tuned config. Our split-K force `log2_ks=3` (8-way) is already applied, but we should verify it's actually reaching the kernel. 

3. **MLA**: At the ASM kernel ceiling. The only remaining avenue would be to find additional `.co` kernel binaries on the runner with different tile configs, but that requires a probe submission.

These are diminishing-returns optimizations within the ASM kernel framework. The fundamental breakthrough would require either:
- Different `.co` kernel binaries not currently available on the runner
- Custom HIP/Triton kernels that beat CK ASM (confirmed dead end for all three)
- New aiter API features not yet discovered

The production files are clean, correct, and at their current performance ceiling.

     1→---
     2→name: gfx950-vgpr-pressure-triton-mla
     3→description: >
     4→  VGPR register pressure analysis for Triton kernels on AMD gfx950.
     5→  Use when: "Triton kernel slow on large tiles", "tl.dot register spilling",
     6→  "BLOCK_M tiling no improvement", "KV bandwidth doubles after head-block tiling".
     7→  Covers MLA FlashDecoding, split-D attention, MFMA register budgets.
     8→author: Claude Code
     9→version: 1.0.0
    10→---
    11→
    12→# gfx950 VGPR Pressure in Triton Kernels
    13→
    14→## Problem
    15→
    16→Triton kernels on AMD gfx950 (MI355X) perform much worse than expected for large tile
    17→sizes. A FlashDecoding MLA kernel with `acc[32, 512]` fp32 accumulator ran 10x slower
    18→than expected on large shapes, despite correct results.
    19→
    20→## Root Cause: KV Tile Dominates VGPR Budget
    21→
    22→On gfx950 with MFMA-16x16 layout:
    23→
    24→| Tensor | Shape | VGPRs per thread | Notes |
    25→|--------|-------|-------------------|-------|
    26→| `acc[32, 512]` fp32 | 32x512 | ~256 | Saturates entire budget |
    27→| `acc[16, 512]` fp32 | 16x512 | ~128 | Half budget (BLOCK_M=16) |
    28→| `kv1[64, 512]` bf16 | 64x512 | ~256 | **The real bottleneck** |
    29→| `q1[16, 512]` bf16 | 16x512 | ~64 | Persists across iterations |
    30→
    31→**Key finding:** Reducing BLOCK_M from 32 to 16 halved the accumulator (256 → 128 VGPRs)
    32→but did NOT improve performance because `kv1[BLOCK_N=64, D1=512]` alone requires ~256
    33→VGPRs during `tl.dot`. The KV tile is the dominant register consumer, not the accumulator.
    34→
    35→## BLOCK_M Tiling Increases KV Bandwidth
    36→
    37→With `nq` heads and BLOCK_M tiling:
    38→- `num_head_blocks = nq / BLOCK_M` blocks per batch element
    39→- Each head block independently loads the same KV data
    40→- Total KV bandwidth multiplied by `nq / BLOCK_M`
    41→
    42→For MLA with nq=32, BLOCK_M=16: 2x KV bandwidth vs BLOCK_M=32.
    43→For bs=256, kv=8192: 2 × 2.3 GB = 4.6 GB KV reads → 3900 µs (vs 370 µs with ref_kernel).
    44→
    45→**Rule:** BLOCK_M tiling trades register pressure for bandwidth. Only beneficial when
    46→shapes are compute-bound (small KV). For bandwidth-bound shapes (large KV), BLOCK_M
    47→tiling is strictly worse.
    48→
    49→## VGPR Budget Calculation (gfx950 MFMA-16x16)
    50→
    51→Per-thread VGPR count for a 2D tensor in MFMA layout:
    52→```
    53→VGPRs = (dim0 / 16) × (dim1 / 16) × 4 values × (bytes_per_elem / 4)
    54→```
    55→
    56→For fp32 (4 bytes): `VGPRs = (M/16) × (N/16) × 4`
    57→For bf16 (2 bytes): `VGPRs = (M/16) × (N/16) × 2` (packed into VGPRs)
    58→
    59→gfx950 limit: 256 VGPRs per thread. Exceeding this causes spilling to scratch memory
    60→(backed by HBM), which is orders of magnitude slower.
    61→
    62→## Optimal Strategy for MLA FlashDecoding
    63→
    64→| Shape regime | Bottleneck | Best approach |
    65→|-------------|-----------|---------------|
    66→| bs*kv ≤ 100k | Kernel launch + Python overhead | Custom FlashDecoding (avoids ref_kernel's ~130 µs fixed overhead) |
    67→| bs*kv > 100k | HBM bandwidth | ref_kernel with fp8 KV (half bandwidth) |
    68→
    69→Custom Triton FlashDecoding with split-D (512+64) wins for small shapes because:
    70→1. No ~130 µs aiter metadata/dispatch overhead
    71→2. Head-packed GEMM (M=16 or M=32, not M=1 GEMV)
    72→3. FlashDecoding split-KV for CU utilization
    73→
    74→## Verification
    75→
    76→Benchmark both paths and set threshold empirically:
    77→```python
    78→if bs * kvseqlen > THRESHOLD:
    79→    return ref_kernel(data)  # fp8 ASM, bandwidth-optimal
    80→return _mla_flash_attention(q, kv_data, bs, kvseqlen, config)
    81→```
    82→
    83→## References
    84→
    85→- MI355X: 304 CUs, 8 XCDs, 256 VGPRs/thread, ~8 TB/s HBM3e
    86→- MFMA-16x16: 4 result elements per thread per tile
    87→- aiter mla_decode_fwd: ~130 µs fixed overhead (metadata + JIT dispatch)
    88→
```

  Critical parameter semantics for aiter GPU kernels (fused_moe, mla_decode_fwd) on AMD MI355X.

  with fast_mode or num_kv_splits, (3) correctness failures after changing aiter MoE or MLA
  parameters, (4) tuning AITER_KSPLIT for sparse vs dense token distributions.
  Key insights: REDACTED changes computation (not just performance) and WILL cause
  correctness failures. fast_mode=True in MLA metadata is SLOWER on MI355X. AITER_KSPLIT
  switches between two kernel implementations — must use adaptive strategy based on
  estimated_m_per_expert < 50 for correct performance. KSPLIT ignored for CSV-tuned shapes.
  Use AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1 to override CSV-locked shapes. KSPLIT=4 outperforms KSPLIT=2
  for very sparse 257E+ expert shapes (num_experts>=200, estimated_m<10).

# aiter Kernel Parameter Semantics (AMD MI355X)

## Problem

aiter's `fused_moe` and `mla_decode_fwd` have parameters that sound like performance flags
but actually change computation or have counter-intuitive effects on MI355X.

## fused_moe: doweight_stage1

### What it does

`REDACTED` moves the `topk_weights` multiplication from **stage 2** (after SiLU
activation) to **stage 1** (before SiLU activation).

- `doweight_stage1=False` (default): `output = SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)`, then multiply weights in stage 2
- `REDACTED`: multiply weights in stage 1 BEFORE activation

### Why it causes correctness failure

SiLU is a **nonlinear** activation. Multiplying weights before vs after a nonlinear activation
produces mathematically different results:

```
w * SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)   ≠   SiLU(w * gate(x)) * (w * up(x))
```

This is not a subtle numerical difference — it produces ~80% element mismatches with 10-30%
per-element error (well above the 5e-2 tolerance for MoE).

**Rule:** `REDACTED` only produces correct results for linear activations (ReLU-like),
not for SiLU/GELU. For MXFP4 MoE with SiLU, always use `doweight_stage1=False`.

## fused_moe: OPUS Sorting (AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING)

Setting `os.environ["AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING"] = "1"` before calling `fused_moe` switches
to the OPUS sorting backend (`moe_sorting_opus_fwd` instead of `moe_sorting_fwd`).

**Correctness:** OPUS sorting only changes token dispatch order — the computation is identical.
Setting this env var passes correctness tests (verified, max error 0.015625).

**Performance on MXFP4 variant:** NO EFFECT. The env var is not wired into the fp4x2 code path
---SKILL2---

  (2) leaderboard leaders are 2x+ faster than your reference-matching submission,
  (3) planning next steps after hitting the aiter/fused_moe/mla_decode_fwd parameter ceiling,
  (4) cross-referencing a past kernel challenge (e.g., VLIW) for applicable patterns.
  Key insight: Top entries bypass library APIs entirely. The "Two Builders" pattern
  (correctness anchor + custom explorer) is the structured path to custom kernel work.

# Competitive Kernel Optimization: Beyond Parameter Tuning

## Problem

After exhausting all tunable parameters in a library (e.g., aiter's `fused_moe`), ranking
stays in the lower half of the leaderboard. The reference implementation is already
well-optimized; parameter sweeps either have no effect, cause correctness failures, or
degrade performance.

## Context: When to Use This

- All accessible API parameters have been tested (documented in `aiter-kernel-parameter-semantics`)
- Still ranked outside top 20 with >2x gap to leader
- Considering whether to abandon vs. continue the challenge
- Wanting to plan next iteration with higher expected value

## The Decision Hierarchy

### Level 1: Local Proxy (Low effort, immediate)

Before expensive remote submissions, build a local model of the performance space:

**Parse aiter's tuned CSV configs:**
```bash
cat /path/to/aiter/aiter/configs/tuned_fmoe.csv
```

# aiter Kernel Parameter Semantics (AMD MI355X)

## Problem

aiter's `fused_moe` and `mla_decode_fwd` have parameters that sound like performance flags
but actually change computation or have counter-intuitive effects on MI355X.

## fused_moe: doweight_stage1

### What it does

`REDACTED` moves the `topk_weights` multiplication from **stage 2** (after SiLU
activation) to **stage 1** (before SiLU activation).

- `doweight_stage1=False` (default): `output = SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)`, then multiply weights in stage 2
- `REDACTED`: multiply weights in stage 1 BEFORE activation

### Why it causes correctness failure

SiLU is a **nonlinear** activation. Multiplying weights before vs after a nonlinear activation
produces mathematically different results:

```
w * SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)   ≠   SiLU(w * gate(x)) * (w * up(x))
```

This is not a subtle numerical difference — it produces ~80% element mismatches with 10-30%
per-element error (well above the 5e-2 tolerance for MoE).

**Rule:** `REDACTED` only produces correct results for linear activations (ReLU-like),
not for SiLU/GELU. For MXFP4 MoE with SiLU, always use `doweight_stage1=False`.

## fused_moe: OPUS Sorting (AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING)

Setting `os.environ["AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING"] = "1"` before calling `fused_moe` switches
to the OPUS sorting backend (`moe_sorting_opus_fwd` instead of `moe_sorting_fwd`).

**Correctness:** OPUS sorting only changes token dispatch order — the computation is identical.
Setting this env var passes correctness tests (verified, max error 0.015625).

**Performance on MXFP4 variant:** NO EFFECT. The env var is not wired into the fp4x2 code path
in `fused_moe.py`. JIT still builds `module_moe_sorting` (not `module_moe_sorting_opus`), and
benchmark times are identical to reference. This was verified by running the full benchmark suite.

## fused_moe: block_size_M

Override the auto-selected tile size for the token dimension:

```python
fused_moe(..., block_size_M=64)  # or 32, 128
```

Default is `-1` (auto-selects from tuned CSV configs). For MXFP4 with different model configs:
- bs=8 (small): auto-selects `block_m=32`
- bs=128 (medium): auto-selects `block_m=64`

**DANGER: Forcing `block_size_M=64` causes GPU memory access faults on benchmark configs.**
When `estimated_m_per_expert` is low (e.g., bs=16 with 256 experts = ~1 token/expert), a tile
size of 64 exceeds the actual data bounds and causes a hardware-level `Memory access fault by
GPU node-2`. The auto-tuner correctly selects smaller tiles for these shapes. **Never override
`block_size_M` without verifying it's safe for ALL shapes in the benchmark suite.**

## mla_decode_fwd: fast_mode is SLOWER on MI355X

### What it does

`fast_mode=True` in `get_mla_metadata_info_v1` and `get_mla_metadata_v1` changes the work
distribution strategy: `(bs + cu_num) * tiles` work items vs `tile_cnt * cu_num`.

### Test results (MI355X, DeepSeek R1 MLA, fp8)

| Config | fast_mode=False (reference) | fast_mode=True, adaptive splits | fast_mode=True, fixed splits=32 |
|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------------|
| bs=4, kv=1024 | ~131 us | ~154 us | ~152 us |
| bs=256, kv=8192 | ~462 us | ~536 us | ~542 us |
| Geomean | ~191 us | ~224 us (+17%) | ~232 us (+21%) |

**fast_mode=True is 17-21% SLOWER than fast_mode=False on MI355X for decode workloads.**

The reference implementation already uses well-tuned defaults. Changing `fast_mode` to `True`
and `num_kv_splits` from 32 to adaptive values (4-16 depending on batch size) both made
performance worse, not better.

**Rule:** For MLA decode on MI355X with the DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb architecture, use:
```python
fast_mode=False
num_kv_splits=32
intra_batch_mode=True
```

## fused_moe: AITER_KSPLIT (Environment Variable)

`AITER_KSPLIT=N` switches `fused_moe` between two entirely different kernel implementations:

- **KSPLIT=0 (default / unset):** Uses `module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_fp4x2_preshuffle_*` — MXFP4-optimized CK kernels, fast for dense token distributions
- **KSPLIT>0:** Uses `module_moe_cktile2stages` — generic cktile kernels (not MXFP4-specialized), but better for sparse token distributions

**Critical:** AITER_KSPLIT is **completely ignored** for shapes with pre-tuned CSV configs (e.g., 257-expert DeepSeek-V3 configs in `tuned_fmoe.csv`). Only affects shapes that fall back to default kernel selection.

### Benchmark Data (MI355X, dhidden=7168, with AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1)

**33-expert shapes (dexp=512/2048):**

| Shape | KSPLIT=0 | KSPLIT=2 | Winner |
|-------|----------|----------|--------|
| bs=16, 33 exp | 94.5 µs | 60.6 µs | cktile (-36%) |
| bs=128, 33 exp | 130 µs | 109 µs | cktile (-16%) |
| bs=512, 33 exp, dexp=512 | 214 µs | 253 µs | CK (+18%) |
| bs=512, 33 exp, dexp=2048 | 352 µs | 703 µs | CK (+100%) |

**257-expert shapes (dexp=256, TP=8 DeepSeek-V3) — BYPASS_TUNE required:**

| Shape | CK (CSV, est_m) | KSPLIT=2 | KSPLIT=4 | Winner |
|-------|---------|----------|----------|--------|
| bs=16, 257E (est_m≈0) | 152.7 µs | 95.1 µs | 91.3 µs | KSPLIT=4 (-40% vs CK) |
| bs=128, 257E (est_m≈4) | 239 µs | 186 µs | 172 µs | KSPLIT=4 (-28% vs CK) |
| bs=512, 257E (est_m≈17) | 336.5 µs | 275 µs | 284 µs | KSPLIT=2 (-18% vs CK) |

**Full 7-shape competition benchmark (geomean target: ~155 µs vs ~145 µs leader):**

| Shape | Reference | Phase 8 (BYPASS+K2) | Phase 10 (expert-aware) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|------------------------|
| bs=16, 257E | 152.7 µs | 95.1 µs | 91.6 µs (KSPLIT=4) |
| bs=128, 257E | 239 µs | 186 µs | 171 µs (KSPLIT=4) |
| bs=512, 257E | 336.5 µs | 275 µs | 284 µs (KSPLIT=2) |
| bs=16, 33E | 106.2 µs | 59.8 µs | 59.8 µs (KSPLIT=2) |
| bs=128, 33E | 141.1 µs | 109 µs | 109 µs (KSPLIT=2) |
| bs=512, 33E dexp=512 | 225 µs | 209 µs | 214 µs (KSPLIT=0) |
| bs=512, 33E dexp=2048 | 380.4 µs | 343 µs | 355 µs (KSPLIT=0) |
| **Geomean** | ~206 µs | ~156 µs | ~155 µs |

**Rule:** `estimated_m_per_expert = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts`
- `estimated_m >= 50` → KSPLIT unset (CK path, dense shapes)
- `num_experts >= 200 AND estimated_m < 10` → KSPLIT=4 (large expert count, tiny per-expert matrices benefit from K-parallelism)
- `estimated_m < 50` (other) → KSPLIT=2 (cktile path, moderate sparse)

**Why KSPLIT=4 helps for 257E but not 33E:** 257E shapes have dexp=256 (small weight matrices per expert). With K=256, KSPLIT=4 splits K into 64-element chunks which fits better in cache. 33E shapes have dexp=512+ (larger matrices where KSPLIT=2 already saturates K-parallelism).

### ⚠️ DANGER: KSPLIT=4 Causes Catastrophic Overflow for 32/33-expert shapes

**Confirmed 2026-03-15:** Widening the KSPLIT=4 threshold to cover all sparse shapes (e.g., `est_m < 100`) causes **catastrophic numerical overflow (~1e27 output values)** for 32/33-expert shapes with dexp=512.

**Root cause:** For 33E/dexp=512, `K = 512` → `K / KSPLIT = 512 / 4 = 128`. The cktile reduction kernel has a minimum effective K-chunk requirement that 128 violates. Result: garbage values, not an error — silently produces ~1e27 where max error should be ~0.015.

**Symptom:** Correctness tests with ONLY 257E shapes pass (max error 0.015625 within 5e-2 tolerance). Benchmarks with 33E shapes produce `1e27`. Easy to miss if test suite doesn't cover all expert topologies.

**Safe KSPLIT=4 condition:** `num_experts >= 200` only (257E topology: dexp=256, K/4=64 safe). For any 32/33-expert shape: KSPLIT ≤ 2.

```python
# SAFE — confirmed correct for all 7 competition shapes
if estimated_m >= 50:
    ks = "default"                              # CK MXFP4 path
elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
    ks = "4"                                    # 257E only — dexp=256, K/4=64 safe
else:
    ks = "2"                                    # 33E — dexp=512, K/4=128 overflows

# DANGEROUS — causes 1e27 for bs=16, 33 experts, dexp=512:
# if estimated_m < 100: ks = "4"   ← NEVER DO THIS
```

### Expert-Count-Aware Adaptive KSPLIT Pattern

Setting env var per-call works because AITER checks it at dispatch time (not module import):

```python
import os

num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts

if estimated_m >= 50:
    # Dense: CK MXFP4-optimized path
    os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
    os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
    # Large expert count, very sparse: KSPLIT=4 for K-parallelism
    os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "4"
else:
    # Moderately sparse or small expert count: KSPLIT=2
    os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"

return fused_moe(...)
```

### Stderr Diagnostics: Reading Kernel Path Selection

The aiter log output tells you definitively which path was taken — critical for debugging KSPLIT behavior:

**CSV-tuned shape (KSPLIT ignored):**
```
[aiter] [fused_moe] using 2stage (kernelName1='moe_ck2stages_gemm1_256x32x128x128_...', kernelName2='...') for (...)
```
The parenthetical `(kernelName1=...)` means a specific CSV-tuned kernel was selected. AITER_KSPLIT has no effect here.

**Default shape (KSPLIT applies):**
```
[aiter] run_1stage = False, ksplit = 2 q_type = QuantType.per_1x32 block_m = 32 use_nt = True, estimated_m_per_expert = 4
[aiter] [fused_moe] using 2stage default for (...)
```
The `using 2stage default` message confirms KSPLIT was read and applied. `ksplit = 2` confirms the cktile path was selected.

**Key log fields:**
- `ksplit = 0` → MXFP4-optimized CK path (KSPLIT unset or =0)
- `ksplit = 2` → cktile path (AITER_KSPLIT=2)
- `use_nt = True` → non-temporal memory loads enabled (AITER_USE_NT env var)
- `estimated_m_per_expert = N` → the computed token density value

### JIT Cost of KSPLIT>0

Enabling KSPLIT>0 for the first time triggers **additional JIT builds**:
- `module_moe_cktile2stages`: ~110s
- `module_activation`: ~22s

Total additional JIT time: ~132s. Combined with base MoE JIT (~128s), this pushes total JIT to ~260s, leaving ~7.5 min of compute time in a 12-min Popcorn workflow.

## fused_moe: AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG (Environment Variable)

`AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1` forces the **heuristic path** for all shapes, even those with pre-tuned CSV configs:

```python
if cfg is None or int(os.environ.get("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", "0")):
    # Heuristic path — AITER_KSPLIT applies here
    ksplit = get_ksplit(token, topk, expert, inter_dim, model_dim)
    ...
```

**Use case:** When CSV-tuned configs exist (e.g., 257-expert DeepSeek TP=8 shapes) but you want to test the cktile (split_k) path for cold-cache benchmarks.

Combined strategy:
```python
if estimated_m < 50:
    os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"  # Ignore CSV for sparse shapes
    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"              # Force cktile path
else:
    os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
    os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)           # CK path for dense
```

**Risk:** CSV configs were pre-tuned (possibly at hot cache). Cold-cache benchmarks may or may not prefer cktile — requires empirical testing.

**AITER_ONLINE_TUNE Warning:** `AITER_ONLINE_TUNE=1` blocks on running `gemm_moe_tune.py` (a full benchmark sweep) before returning for the FIRST call. This adds many minutes to the first call and will TIMEOUT a 12-minute workflow.

## fused_moe: cktile Path Trigger Conditions

The cktile kernels (`cktile_moe_stage1` + `cktile_moe_stage2`) are used instead of CK when ALL of:
1. `dtype in [bf16, fp16]`
2. `q_type == QuantType.per_1x32` (MXFP4)
3. `q_dtype_w in [dtypes.fp4x2]` (MXFP4 weights)
4. **`ksplit > 1`** — set by AITER_KSPLIT or get_ksplit()
5. **`is_shuffled=True`** — requires pre-shuffled weights (shuffled variants in submission)

If any condition is false, the CK path is used. Using `gate_up_weight_shuffled` and `down_weight_shuffled` (shuffled=True) is required for cktile.

## fused_moe: Parameter Quick Reference

| Parameter | Default | Effect | Safe to Change? |
|-----------|---------|--------|----------------|
| `doweight_stage1` | `False` | **Changes computation** — must be `False` for SiLU | NO (SiLU fails) |
| `block_size_M` | `-1` (auto) | Changes CK tile size | **DANGEROUS** — GPU memory fault if too large for shape |
| `moe_sorting_dispatch_policy` | `0` | Token dispatch strategy | Yes but 20-79% SLOWER at policy=1 |
| `AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING` | unset | Dispatch backend | No effect on MXFP4 variant |
| `AITER_KSPLIT` | unset (=0) | **Switches kernel implementation** — see above | Yes (adaptive per estimated_m) |
| `AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG` | unset | Ignore CSV pre-tuned configs, use heuristic | Yes (use with KSPLIT=2 for CSV-locked shapes) |
| `AITER_ONLINE_TUNE` | unset | Block on tuning run before first call | **NO** — will timeout 12-min workflow |

## mla_decode_fwd: Parameter Quick Reference

| Parameter | Reference Value | Effect | Change? |
|-----------|----------------|--------|---------|
| `fast_mode` | `False` | Work distribution strategy | NO — slower on MI355X |
| `num_kv_splits` | `32` | Split count for KV attention | Tested 4-16, all slower |
| `intra_batch_mode` | `True` | Intra-batch parallelism | Do not change |
| `page_size` | `1` | KV page size | Do not change |

## aiter CSV Config File Structure

aiter stores pre-tuned kernel configurations in CSV files under `<aiter_path>/configs/`. These are **merged at startup** and determine which shapes are "CSV-tuned" (AITER_KSPLIT ignored) vs "default" (AITER_KSPLIT applies):

| File | Contents | Quant Type |
|------|----------|------------|
| `tuned_fmoe.csv` | Generic GPU configs (cu_num=80), bf16/fp8/int8 | NOT MXFP4 |
| `dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv` | DeepSeek-V3 257-expert MXFP4 configs | `per_1x32` (MXFP4) |
| `a8w8_blockscale_tuned_fmoe_qwen3_235b.csv` | Qwen3 configs | int8 block-scale |
| `untuned_fmoe.csv` | Shapes needing tuning (not yet profiled) | various |

**Critical implication:** The 257-expert DeepSeek shapes used by the Luma AMD benchmark are tuned via `dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv`. AITER_KSPLIT is completely ignored for these shapes. Only 33-expert or other non-DeepSeek shapes fall through to the "default" path where AITER_KSPLIT applies.

### Introspection Probe Stderr Truncation Warning

When dumping aiter CSV configs in a Popcorn submission (via stderr print), **large CSV files overflow the stderr buffer** and truncate subsequent output. To avoid this when introspecting:

1. **Never dump all CSVs** — filter to only the target file (e.g., `'dsv3_fp4' in f.lower()`)
2. **Separate probes for CSVs vs source code** — run CSV dump probe and source-code probe as separate submissions
3. **Print source code first** — if you must combine, print `inspect.getsource()` before CSV content

remains in the 12-min workflow for actual parameter sweep testing.

The core finding was highly non-obvious and counter-intuitive: the hypothesis that "always use A16W8 to skip Q quantization" seemed sound from a bandwidth analysis perspective (Q is tiny, 3 quantization kernels cost ~3µs, Q fp8 bandwidth saving is ~0.75µs, net gain expected). But empirically it caused a **+289µs regression at bs=256, kv=8192**.

The actual reason: the two `.co` kernel binaries have completely different tile architectures. `mla_a8w8_qh16_qseqlen1_gqaratio16` includes GQA-ratio-16 awareness in its tile layout, making it ~2x faster for large decode batches — a property independent of quantization cost.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The hidden variable in kernel selection**: When aiter selects between kernel binaries, the `q_scale` parameter acts as more than just a "use quantized Q" flag — it's a complete kernel binary switch. The two binaries were compiled with different tile configs targeting different workload regimes. This means benchmarking bandwidth savings alone is insufficient; you must measure the combined effect including the tile config change.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

def _choose_num_kv_splits(total_kv: int) -> int:
    """Adaptive split-K factor - optimized for MI355X."""
    if total_kv <= 4096:
        return 1       # No splitting for tiny KV
    if total_kv <= 32768:
        return 2
    if total_kv <= 131072:
        return 4
    if total_kv <= 524288:
        return 8
    return 16  # Not 32!  Reduced overhead
```

**Comparison to Workspace:**
- `opencode_workspace/mla/submission.py:26` uses fixed `NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32`
- `opencode_infinity/mla/submission.py:35-46` uses adaptive with **max 16** for large KV

**Key Difference:** You may be using `num_kv_splits=16` or `32` universally. The infinity team uses `_choose_num_kv_splits()` to reduce it for small KV (4-element reduction in split count for `total_kv <= 131072`).

### **6. CUDA Graph Capture for MoE**

**Files:**

# Warmup 3 times
for _ in range(3):
    fused_moe(...)  # JIT compilation happens here

```

@triton.jit
def _mla_paged_v17_kernel(..., BLOCK_S=128, BLOCK_D=1024):
    # Online softmax with rolling maximum
    m_i = -float('inf')
    l_i = 0.0
    acc = tl.zeros([BLOCK_D], dtype=tl.float32)
    
    for start_s in range(0, kv_len, BLOCK_S):
        # Flash attention update
        m_next = tl.maximum(m_i, m_ij)
        alpha = tl.exp(m_i - m_next)
        beta = tl.exp(m_ij - m_next)
        acc = acc * alpha + ...
```

def _select_block_m_xcd(num_tokens, topk, num_experts, inter_dim):
    """XCD-aware block_m selection for MI355X."""
    # Normal heuristic
    for bm in candidates:
        rounds = (tg_num + _CU_NUM - 1) // _CU_NUM
        empty = _CU_NUM - (tg_num % _CU_NUM) if tg_num % _CU_NUM else 0
        
        # XCD ALIGNMENT BONUS (NEW!)
        xcd_alignment = 0.0
        if num_experts <= 8 and num_tokens >= 512:
            tokens_per_expert = (num_tokens * topk) // num_experts
            if tokens_per_expert % 8 == 0:  # Perfectly divisible by XCD count
                xcd_alignment = -0.5  # Bonus to score
        
        score = (rounds, empty + xcd_alignment, bm)
```

**Line Details:**
- `a3_optimized.py:115-120` - XCD alignment bonus when tokens/expert divides evenly by 8

**Your Current:** Likely using standard CU occupancy without XCD awareness.

### **10. GEMM: XCD-Aware Chunk Mapping**

**Files:**
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/gemini_squad/breakthroughs/gemm_graph_18us.py` (Lines 99-117)

**Untried Logic:**
```python
num_chunks = tl.cdiv(total_blocks_M * total_blocks_N, CHUNK_SIZE)
chunk_id = pid // CHUNK_SIZE
chunk_lane = pid % CHUNK_SIZE
xcd_id = chunk_id % NUM_XCDS  # 8 on MI355X
remapped_chunk = xcd_id * tl.cdiv(num_chunks, NUM_XCDS) + (chunk_id // NUM_XCDS)
remapped_pid = remapped_chunk * CHUNK_SIZE + chunk_lane
```

**What This Does:**
- Groups thread blocks into chunks
- Assigns chunks to XCDs (8 on MI355X)
- Each XCD processes its assigned chunks
- Better L2 cache locality

**Lines:** `gemm_graph_18us.py:103-108` - Full remapping logic

```python
GRAPH_CACHE = {}

def custom_kernel(data):
    # Graph key by input tensor pointers
    key = (A.data_ptr(), B.data_ptr())
    if key in GRAPH_CACHE:
        graph, output = GRAPH_CACHE[key]
        graph.replay()
        return output
    
    # Capture graph once
    g = torch.cuda.CUDAGraph()
    with torch.cuda.graph(g):
        # Fused quant + GEMM
        a_fp4, a_scale = _mxfp4_quant_op(...)
        # ... GEMM kernel
    
    GRAPH_CACHE[key] = (g, output)
    return output
```

**Savings:** Eliminates quantization + GEMM dispatch overhead on replay (~5-10µs).

    57→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    58→_buf_cache: dict = {}
    59→_param_cache: dict = {}  # NEW: Cache for block_m, split_k, padding
    60→_scale_cache: dict = {}  # NEW: Cache for scale views
    61→_fallback_ks: dict = {"v": None}
    62→
    63→
    64→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    65→# Parameter selection (cached results)
    66→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    67→def _select_block_m(num_tokens: int, topk: int, num_experts: int, inter_dim: int) -> int:
    68→    """Compute optimal block_m using CU occupancy heuristic."""
    69→    tile_n = 128
    70→    tg_n = (inter_dim + tile_n - 1) // tile_n
    71→    candidates = [32, 64, 128]
    72→    best = (float("inf"), float("inf"), 32)
    73→    for bm in candidates:
    74→        max_tokens_padded = num_tokens * topk + num_experts * bm - topk
    75→        tg_num = tg_n * ((max_tokens_padded + bm - 1) // bm)
    76→        rounds = (tg_num + _CU_NUM - 1) // _CU_NUM
    77→        empty = _CU_NUM - (tg_num % _CU_NUM) if tg_num % _CU_NUM else 0
    78→        score = (rounds, empty, bm)
    79→        if score < best:
    80→            best = score
    81→    return best[2]
    82→
    83→
    84→def _select_split_k(estimated_m: int, num_experts: int, d_hidden: int, d_expert: int) -> int:
    85→    """Choose split_k based on shape characteristics."""
    86→    if num_experts >= 128:
    87→        if estimated_m < 32:
    88→            return 4
    89→        return 2
    90→    if estimated_m >= 128:
    91→        return 0
    92→    if estimated_m >= 32:
    93→        return 2 if d_hidden >= 4096 else 4
    94→    return 4
    95→
    96→
    97→def _compute_padding(hidden_pad: int, intermediate_pad: int) -> tuple:
    98→    """Compute CK tile padding values."""
    99→    n_pad_stage1 = (intermediate_pad // 64) * 64 * 2
   100→    k_pad_stage1 = (hidden_pad // 128) * 128
   101→    n_pad_stage2 = (hidden_pad // 64) * 64
   102→    k_pad_stage2 = (intermediate_pad // 128) * 128
   103→    return (n_pad_stage1, k_pad_stage1, n_pad_stage2, k_pad_stage2)
   104→
   105→
   106→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   107→# Cached parameter retrieval
   108→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   109→def _get_cached_params(
   110→    M: int,
   111→    topk: int,
   112→    num_experts: int,
   113→    model_dim: int,
   114→    inter_dim_packed: int,
   115→    hidden_pad: int,
   116→    intermediate_pad: int,
   117→) -> tuple:
   118→    """Get cached parameters or compute and cache them."""
   119→    key = (M, topk, num_experts, model_dim, inter_dim_packed)
   120→

     7→def e8m0_unshuffle(scale_shuffled, orig_m, orig_n):
     8→    sm, sn = scale_shuffled.shape
     9→    scale = scale_shuffled.view(sm // 32, sn // 8, 4, 16, 2, 2)
    10→    scale = scale.permute(0, 5, 3, 1, 4, 2).contiguous()
    11→    scale = scale.view(sm, sn)
    12→    return scale[:orig_m, :orig_n]
    13→
    14→@triton.jit
    15→def _mxfp4_quant_op(
    16→    x,
    17→    BLOCK_SIZE_N,
    18→    BLOCK_SIZE_M,
    19→    MXFP4_QUANT_BLOCK_SIZE,
    20→):
    21→    EXP_BIAS_FP32: tl.constexpr = 127
    22→    EXP_BIAS_FP4: tl.constexpr = 1
    23→    EBITS_F32: tl.constexpr = 8
    24→    EBITS_FP4: tl.constexpr = 2
    25→    MBITS_F32: tl.constexpr = 23
    26→    MBITS_FP4: tl.constexpr = 1
    27→
    28→    max_normal: tl.constexpr = 6
    29→    min_normal: tl.constexpr = 1
    30→
    31→    NUM_QUANT_BLOCKS: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_SIZE_N // MXFP4_QUANT_BLOCK_SIZE
    32→    x = tl.reshape(x, [BLOCK_SIZE_M, NUM_QUANT_BLOCKS, MXFP4_QUANT_BLOCK_SIZE])
    33→    
    34→    amax = tl.max(tl.abs(x), axis=-1, keep_dims=True)
    35→    amax = amax.to(tl.int32, bitcast=True)
    36→    amax = (amax + 0x200000).to(tl.uint32, bitcast=True) & 0xFF800000
    37→    amax = amax.to(tl.float32, bitcast=True)
    38→    scale_e8m0_unbiased = tl.log2(amax).floor() - 2
    39→    scale_e8m0_unbiased = tl.clamp(scale_e8m0_unbiased, min=-127, max=127)
    40→
    41→    bs_e8m0 = scale_e8m0_unbiased.to(tl.uint8) + 127
    42→    quant_scale = tl.exp2(-scale_e8m0_unbiased)
    43→    qx = x * quant_scale
    44→    qx = qx.to(tl.uint32, bitcast=True)
    45→
    46→    s = qx & 0x80000000
    47→    qx = qx ^ s
    48→
    49→    qx_fp32 = qx.to(tl.float32, bitcast=True)
    50→    saturate_mask = qx_fp32 >= max_normal

    13→import triton.language as tl
    14→from task import input_t, output_t

    16→
    17→@triton.jit
    18→def moe_fused_kernel(
    19→    # Input
    20→    hidden_ptr,
    21→    # Weights (MXFP4 packed)
    22→    w1_ptr,
    23→    w2_ptr,
    24→    # Scales (E8M0)
    25→    w1_scale_ptr,
    26→    w2_scale_ptr,
    27→    # Routing
    28→    topk_ids_ptr,
    29→    topk_weights_ptr,
    30→    # Output
    31→    output_ptr,
    32→    # Dimensions
    33→    M,
    34→    N,
    35→    K,
    36→    topk,
    37→    num_experts,
    38→    # Strides
    39→    stride_hidden_m,
    40→    stride_hidden_k,
    41→    stride_w1_e,
    42→    stride_w1_k,
    43→    stride_w1_n,
    44→    stride_w2_e,
    45→    stride_w2_k,
    46→    stride_w2_n,
    47→    stride_out_m,
    48→    stride_out_n,
    49→    # Block sizes
    50→    BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
    51→    BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
    52→    BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,
    53→):
    54→    """
    55→    Fused MoE kernel - ALL stages in one launch
    56→
    57→    Each thread block handles BLOCK_M tokens × BLOCK_N output features
    58→    """
    59→    # Get program IDs
    60→    pid_m = tl.program_id(0)
    61→    pid_n = tl.program_id(1)
    62→
    63→    # Token offsets for this block
    64→    offs_m = pid_m * BLOCK_M + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_M)
    65→    offs_n = pid_n * BLOCK_N + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_N)
    66→
    67→    # Masks
    68→    mask_m = offs_m < M
    69→    mask_n = offs_n < N
    70→
    71→    # Initialize accumulator
    72→    acc = tl.zeros((BLOCK_M, BLOCK_N), dtype=tl.float32)
    73→
    74→    # For each token in this block
    75→    for tm in range(BLOCK_M):
    76→        m_idx = offs_m[tm]
    77→        if m_idx >= M:
    78→            continue
    79→
    80→        # Load topk experts for this token
    81→        # topk_ids: [M, topk]
    82→        expert_ids = tl.load(

    85→            other=0,

    88→        # Load routing weights

    90→        routing_weights = tl.load(

    93→            other=0.0,
    94→        )
    95→
    96→        # For each expert in topk
    97→        for tk in range(topk):
    98→            expert_id = expert_ids[tk]
    99→            weight = routing_weights[tk]

Now I have the full picture. Let me address your excellent question about Rust/assembly and design the new plan.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why "Rust or assembly" is the right instinct but needs reframing:**
1. The GPU kernels ARE already hand-tuned assembly — aiter's `.co` (code object) files are CK team's optimized ISA for gfx950. We can't beat those.
2. The bottleneck isn't GPU compute — it's **Python host-side dispatch**. Each `fused_moe()` call has 5+ kernel launches with Python argument validation, env var parsing, dict lookups, and pybind11 transitions.
3. **Rust** can't work on the runner (no `cargo`), BUT **C++ via `amdclang++` is already proven** — our GEMM submission compiles a HIP kernel at import time. We can extend this to write a C++ dispatch layer that calls aiter's internal functions directly via `dlopen`/`dlsym`, eliminating ALL Python overhead.
4. The Infinity team's `a1_optimized.py` shows the **direct CK dispatch pattern** that bypasses `fused_moe()` entirely — calling `moe_sorting_fwd` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm1/2` directly. This is the "assembly dispatch" equivalent in Python.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Now let me design the breakthrough plan.

    68→- **What**: Replace `fused_moe()` with direct kernel calls, bypassing Python dispatch overhead
    69→- **Pipeline**: `moe_sorting_fwd()` → `fused_dynamic_mxfp4_quant_moe_sort()` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm1()` → `silu_and_mul()` → `moe_cktile2stages_gemm2()`
    70→- **Reference**: `opencode_infinity/teams/gamma/agents/g3/submission.py` (lines 264-397)
    71→- **CRITICAL DIFFERENCES from Infinity**:
    72→  - Use `split_k=2` max (not their `split_k=4` which overflows on 257-expert shapes)

    75→- **CU-aware block_m selection** (from Infinity, validated approach):
    76→  ```python
    77→  def _select_block_m(M, topk, num_experts, inter_dim):
    78→      tile_n = 128
    79→      tg_n = (inter_dim + tile_n - 1) // tile_n
    80→      for bm in [32, 64, 128]:
    81→          max_padded = M * topk + num_experts * bm - topk
    82→          tg_num = tg_n * ((max_padded + bm - 1) // bm)
    83→          rounds = (tg_num + CU_NUM - 1) // CU_NUM
    84→          empty = CU_NUM - (tg_num % CU_NUM) if tg_num % CU_NUM else 0
    85→          # minimize (rounds, empty, bm)
    86→  ```
    87→- **Expected gain**: 3-8µs (eliminates fused_moe Python overhead + smarter block_m)
    88→- **Risk**: HIGH — buffer sizing must be exact or GPU memory fault
    89→- **File**: `kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`

   141→### Task 3.1: `fast_mode=True` A/B test (HIGHEST PRIORITY — never tested)
   142→- [ ] Current production uses `fast_mode=False` in both `get_mla_metadata_info_v1()` and `get_mla_metadata_v1()`
   143→- [ ] Both Gemini and Infinity teams use `fast_mode=True`
   144→- **Change**: Flip to `True` in both metadata calls in `_build_cache()`
   145→- **Expected gain**: 2-5µs (reduces metadata computation overhead)
   146→- **Risk**: LOW — two independent teams validate this works
   147→- **File**: `kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py` lines 69, 95
   148→
   149→### Task 3.2: MXFP4 KV dequant→fp8 for large shapes (EXPERIMENTAL)
   150→- **What**: For bandwidth-bound shapes (bs≥64, kv=8192), dequant mxfp4 KV to fp8 on the fly before passing to `mla_decode_fwd`
   151→- **Rationale**: MXFP4 KV is 4x smaller than bf16 in memory. Even with a dequant step, net bandwidth savings could help large shapes where memory is the bottleneck
   152→- **Implementation**:
   153→  ```python
   154→  kv_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = kv_data["mxfp4"]
   155→  # Dequant: fp4x2 → bf16 → fp8 (via aiter utilities)
   156→  kv_bf16 = dequantize_mxfp4(kv_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4, orig_shape)
   157→  kv_fp8, kv_scale = quantize_fp8(kv_bf16)
   158→  # Then pass to mla_decode_fwd as usual
   159→  ```
   160→- **Why this might NOT work**: The dequant step (fp4→bf16→fp8) adds compute that may negate bandwidth savings. The KV data is already provided in fp8 format (`kv_data["fp8"]`), so re-deriving fp8 from mxfp4 only helps if the mxfp4 data is in GPU memory and fp8 data is not (unlikely — both are in `kv_data` dict)
   161→- **Expected gain**: Uncertain — may be 0 or negative. Test on bs=256/kv=8192 first
   162→- **Fallback**: If dequant overhead > bandwidth savings, abandon this path
   163→- **BLOCKED IF**: `mla_decode_fwd` doesn't accept the re-quantized fp8 format correctly
   164→
   165→### Task 3.3: Adaptive num_kv_splits refinement
   166→- **Current schedule**: `total_kv ≤4096→1, ≤32768→4, ≤131072→8, else→16`
   167→- **Test**: More granular splits for medium shapes (bs=32/kv=8k at 90µs)
   168→- **Expected gain**: 1-3µs
   169→
   170→### Task 3.4: CUDA graph capture for aiter path (MEDIUM RISK)
   171→- **What**: Capture `mla_decode_fwd` + reduce in a CUDA graph for repeated shapes
   172→- **Prior finding**: CUDA graphs regressed GEMM by +78% (copy_() overhead)
   173→- **MLA difference**: MLA inputs (q, kv) are different per call but metadata buffers are static
   174→- **Key insight**: Only graph the ASM kernel + reduce, NOT the q quantization
   175→- **Expected gain**: 3-8µs on large shapes (eliminate Python dispatch for repeated metadata)
   176→- **Risk**: HIGH — may regress like GEMM did
   177→
